Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Preview  |  October 2026

### Page 7

TO P I C  2 | C L I MATE F I N AN C E
Climate finance delivery will be a central test for COP31
June’s Bonn preparatory talks reportedly made little progress and exposed persistent finance divisions, leaving COP31 to turn both the $300bn and $1.3tn-by-2035 goals into a more credible
delivery plan. This is a big ask and requires clearer public/private roles, consistent tracking mechanisms, more concessional finance, risk-sharing and guarantees that can help reduce the cost of

capital. New large-scale finance commitments appear unlikely, with the EU already stating it will not make new pledges.
7
Climate finance: needs, commitments and progress
The image below explains the various climate finance estimates and goals.
Total estimated climate investment need for
developing countries (excluding China)
(annual, by 2035)
$3.2 trillion
per year by 2035
~$1.9 trillion
Domestic finance (within developing countries)
$1.3 trillion

International climate finance (from abroad) –
aligned with the NCQG $1.3tn goal
Source: Independent High-Level Expert Group on Climate
Finance (IHLEG)
$1.3 trillion a year by 2035
COP31 focus: first implementation update on the Baku-Belem
roadmap to $1.3T. This total is inclusive of the $300bn NCQG target.
Context: COP29 set the New Collective Quantified Goal (NCQG),
calling on all actors to scale climate finance for developing countries
to at least $1.3tn a year by 2035. No country has an assigned share,

and private capital is expected to count.
At COP31: first implementation update on the Baku-Belem roadmap
to $1.3T, based on “measurable progress” against targets including:
• a global monitoring framework to track progress in climate finance
mobilization, identify bottlenecks, and highlight emerging solutions.
• clear pathways for public, private, domestic and international
finance;
• Expanding operability, comparability, and interoperability of

climate-finance data; and
• action on grants and concessional finance, debt and fiscal space,
private-finance costs, capacity and system reform.
Are the NCQG and IHLEG aligned? IHLEG’s $1.3tn external-finance
estimate (within the $3.2tn needed excl. China) does align with the
NCQG $1.3tn goal and underpins the Baku-to-Belém Roadmap.
However, they are built differently: political target vs needs-based
estimate.
$300 billion a year by 2035
COP31 focus: what counts and who contributes. This amount

contributes towards the $1.3tn NCQG target from all actors.
Context: Developed countries agreed to lead mobilisation of at least
$300bn a year by 2035, counting public finance and the private
finance it mobilises; no country has a fixed share.
At COP31: Focus turns to how to deliver. Developing countries are
pushing for the development of action plans to chart a pathway to
$300bn and for the Article 9 work programme to be included on the
formal agenda (with emphasis on Article 9.1 – which explicitly covers

developed country finance provision).
Adaptation finance
COP31 focus: embedding and tracking the tripling pledge
Context: COP30 called for adaptation finance to at least triple by
2035, yet this was relatively vague; it did not specify a base year or a
target figure (some have suggested $120bn, i.e. 3x the 2025 goal).
At COP31: Parties need to resolve disagreement on whether to
anchor the pledge in the Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA) and put a
finer point on the agreed Belém Adaptation Indicators that will track

it, after disagreement stalled the June Bonn talks.
Loss and Damage Fund
COP31 focus: guidance, replenishment and access
Why it matters: The Fund is the only dedicated channel for
irreversible climate impacts, yet it has no annual funding target and
resources remain far below demand.
At COP31: Focus will be on its long-term funding strategy, with the
first replenishment cycle set to begin in 2027. $342m made available
in the first funding window, which offers $5–20m grants with at least

half reserved for SIDS and LDCs; but the voluntary nature of
payments, disputes over who pays and sensitivity around
“compensation” make major new pledges in Antalya unlikely.

### Page 8

TO P I C  3 | AD AP TATI O N  &  R ES I L I EN C E (1  o f 2 )
Adaptation will be one of the most important, and more divisive, negotiating topics
Adaptation is also set to be on the formal negotiating agenda at COP31. UN warnings that an overshoot of the Paris Agreement’s 1.5°C goal is now unavoidable (at least temporarily), have
dampened morale for climate activists. As it relates to the COP agenda on adaptation, parties appear deadlocked on whether the COP30 goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035 should be

included in the Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA) text. This is important, as anchoring it to the GGA could signal to investors the scale and timing of future public and blended finance flows.
The role of adaptation in the COP process
The Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA) was established under the Paris Agreement to enhance
adaptive capacity, strengthen resilience and reduce vulnerability to climate change. At COP30
last year, countries adopted 59 indicators (still being refined) to measure progress against the

goal. They also agreed a goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035. The pledge was vague: it set
neither a concrete figure nor a baseline year (a potential annual target of $120bn would be
triple the previous goal of c.$40bn by 2025).
Why GGA talks were deadlocked at the June climate meetings
Finance was the main sticking point at the June Bonn meetings. Developing countries wanted
the COP30 goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035 referenced in the GGA text; countries

including Canada, Norway and Japan opposed. Parties also split on whether the indicators task
force should be expert- or party-led, so no GGA text was agreed and the decision passes to
COP31. Moving the Adaptation Fund to serve the Paris Agreement exclusively (funded by a 5%
share of proceeds from Article 6.4 credits) stalled in Bonn over the composition of its board;
talks will resume at COP31.
What is needed at COP31 to unlock adaptation progress

Adaptation is on the provisional COP31 agenda, including the tripling adaptation finance goal
(though this may get blocked). Some parties want it explicitly referenced in the final COP31
GGA decision; this should become clearer when the agenda is adopted as COP31 opens on 9
Nov. At issue: the tripling pledge still lacks a baseline and named contributors. At COP30,
developed nations resisted new finance obligations, while the EU and many others pushed for

a roadmap to transition away from fossil fuels, which a group of mostly oil- and gas-producing
countries blocked. The result was a looser tripling goal and no agreed fossil-fuel roadmap.
Developing countries are unlikely to give ground, as adaptation relies far more on public
funding than mitigation, so a repeat of COP30’s finance vs. mitigation debate is plausible.
Sources: United Nations, UNEP, Carbon Brief, European Parliament (COP30 outcomes briefing, December 2025)

### Page 9

TO P I C  3 | AD AP TATI O N  &  R ES I L I EN C E (2  o f 2 )
The EU’s proposed Climate Resilience Law plans for a 3°C world, while reiterating commitment to net zero 2050
The EU Commission’s recently proposed Climate Resilience Law would set a reference global warming trajectory of 3°C, providing a benchmark for member states’ adaptation planning. The EU
states it remains committed to reaching net zero by 2050, but some at COP will likely view the proposal as accepting overshoot. With the EU also pointing to an estimated need for EUR70bn in

annual adaptation investment, this provides an interesting backdrop as COP31 seeks to resume adaptation negotiations which stalled earlier this year, primarily due to finance.
What the Commission proposed
On 27 October the Commission presented its EU Framework for Climate Resilience, anchored
by a proposed European Climate Resilience Law. With current global policy commitments
pointing to around 2°C of warming by 2050 and 3°C by 2100, the law would set a common

reference trajectory of 1.5°C in 2030, 2°C in 2050 and 3°C in 2100, with this regarded as the
“baseline assumption”. Member states would be required to use it in national climate risk
assessments and adopt a single national resilience plan by end-2030.
Why it matters: the EU is planning for overshoot
The EU highlighted warnings that overshoot of the 1.5°C goal is now “unavoidable” (even if just
temporarily) as necessitating policy action, even as the EU says it is still committed to climate

neutrality by 2050. The Commission links the plan to Europe’s hottest summer on record and
estimates annual economic losses from climate extremes of €50bn over the past four years,
adding that output losses caused by climate change go far beyond this. It says EU adaptation
needs are ~€70bn a year through to 2050, with Europe warming at 2x the global rate.
What it means for COP31
The proposal demonstrates that warnings on the implications of a significant overshoot of the

Paris Agreement global warming limits are moving from scientific theory to real government
policy. While the opening line of the new EU framework emphasized it remains committed to
reaching net zero by 2050, some may view a 3°C planning baseline as accepting overshoot.
The headline €70bn annual EU adaptation finance requirement also provides an interesting
backdrop heading into Global Goal on Adaptation talks given they are stalled largely due to

disagreement over explicit links to finance in the GGA text. For context, the UNEP estimates
annual adaptation needs for developing countries of $310–365bn by 2035. Ultimately, we
expect adaptation focus to continue growing as warming impacts are increasingly felt and
societal calls for relief grow louder. Corporates will need to consider potential strategic
impacts, while some investors have already begun raising their baseline warming forecasts.

### Page 10

TO P I C  4 | EL EC TR I F I C ATI O N
Türkiye is championing a voluntary pledge to lift electrification from 23% to 35% by 2035
Electrification is expected to be at the centre of this “implementation COP”, targeting a rise in electricity’s share of global final energy use from 23% to 35% by 2035. We expect support for this
action given it is being positioned alongside energy security, but we also expect discussion and debate over how it is delivered (e.g., the role of renewables, nuclear and natural gas), alongside

concerns over clean tech dependency in supply chains. China and the EU have expressed support but not yet committed.
Some may brand this the electrification COP
The Action Agenda pledge aims to raise electricity's share of global final energy from 23%
today to 35% by 2035. The pledge was launched in Bonn this past June and formalised as the
"35-by-35 Global Electrification Pledge" in New York on 21 September.
China and the EU have signaled support, but have not yet signed the pledge

Electrification fits modern day energy-security goals making it a viable target for this year’s
COP. China’s cleantech dominance means it has a further incentive to support initiatives that
seek to increase the pace of global electrification. It also aligns closely with China's existing
development trajectory. While the 35% goal is based on Paris-aligned pathways supported by
IEA and IRENA analysis, the numerical target itself does not require the additional electricity to

come from non fossil fuel sources. This has invited some criticism, though it may make it
easier for some nations to sign the non-binding pledge.
Alignment with the voluntary renewables pledge
Recall that COP28 called for tripling global renewable power capacity by 2030 in COP28’s
formal Global Stocktake decision, equivalent to around 11.2 TW. IRENA’s September 2026
assessment puts capacity at 5.15 TW at end-2025. Record additions of 693 GW in 2025 remain

below the 1,200 GW needed annually in 2026–30. The 35-by-35 pledge complements this
supply goal by shifting transport, buildings and industry towards electricity, enabling
renewable power to replace fossil fuels. Its emissions benefit still depends on cleaner
generation.
We view the goal positively, but delivery needs grid enhancements.
IRENA counts ~2,500 GW of renewable, storage and large-load projects awaiting connection
and says annual investment to strengthen grid infrastructure and enhance system flexibility

must rise from ~$500bn in 2025 to around $1tn through 2035, and $1.2tn thereafter. That
said, signatories signal support, but domestic delivery plans will determine investment.
Sources: UNFCCC, COP31 Presidency, IEA, IRENA

TO P I C  5   |   F O S S I L  F U EL S
The fossil fuel transition roadmap track is growing outside the formal COP process
COP30 failed to agree a fossil fuel transition roadmap, despite support from more than 80 countries. Brazil’s COP30 presidency is therefore developing a roadmap outside formal UN
negotiations to be presented ahead of COP31. Separately, Colombia and the Netherlands convened a ‘coalition of the willing’ of 57 countries in Santa Marta in April 2026 to explore practical

steps towards a fossil fuel phase-out. This parallel forum allows willing countries to cooperate without needing consensus from all COP parties.
Sources: Carbon Brief, Santa Marta conference report

TO P I C  6 | TR AD E
Trade could resurface at COP31 as a challenge to collective agreement; talks have been requested
Trade tensions could feature at COP31 as governments weigh climate ambition against industrial competitiveness. Developing countries fear that measures such as the CBAM disadvantage

their exporters, while the EU says they prevent carbon leakage. COP30 agreed three annual dialogues on trade's role in climate cooperation (no binding outcome), creating for the first time a
formal space for trade discussions in the UNFCCC process, but The African Group and others would like it on the formal COP31 agenda.
Do trade discussions belong at COP?
Developing countries have long pushed back against climate-related trade measures such as
the EU's Carbon Border Adjustment Mechanism (CBAM) and Deforestation Regulation (EUDR).

They argue such measures are protectionism disguised as climate policy and
disproportionately impact poorer countries. Developed countries view such measures as
necessary to level the playing field for domestic firms in cases where foreign competitors face
lower environment-related compliance costs relative to domestic industries, while also arguing
that COP is not the appropriate forum to discuss trade matters. Despite this, a European
Parliament briefing noted trade is a point of “exposure” for the EU at COP31.

COP30’s agreement on trade dialogue was notable, but it may not be enough
COP30 agreed three annual climate-trade dialogues at the mid-year sessions in 2026-28, with
a high-level event in 2028. The first, in Bonn (13 June), was calmer than expected but exposed
the divide: developing countries argued that climate-linked trade measures such as CBAM
raise costs and restrict market access and called for impact assessments within the UNFCCC,
while the EU defended them as responses to environmental externalities. With trade also

raised in other Bonn rooms, it is far from clear the dialogue will contain the issue in Antalya.
The African Group of negotiators has requested a Trade dialogue on the formal agenda.
China has already said it intends to use COP31 to oppose cleantech trade barriers, with its vice
minister of the Ministry of Ecology and Environment saying the event should discuss “in depth”
the “new problem of unilateralism and protectionism undermining global collective efforts” on

climate. The African Group is also pushing for trade to be on the formal agenda. Ultimately,
trade talks could prove more consequential at COP31 than in previous years given inflationary
pressures and concerns over business competitiveness, but it is hard to assess the potential
impact other than possibly causing delays in agreement. However, even if the impact is
limited, the issue could shape negotiations elsewhere outside of the COP process.
Sources: European Parliament, Carbon Brief, Reuters

### Page 13

TOP I C 7 | G EO P O L I TI CS  & MU L TI L ATER AL I S M (1  o f 2 )
Multilateralism is at a crossroads
Geopolitical fragmentation and the US exit from the Paris Agreement make consensus among nearly 200 nations harder, encouraging “coalitions of the willing” (outside of the formal COP
negotiations) to pursue more ambitious climate goals. COP31’s success or failure in securing multilateral agreement or credible voluntary pledges could shape future COPs by showing which

approaches deliver progress. The US retreat from the Paris Agreement will also continue to have far-reaching implications for climate finance. Overall, the EU and China will be expected to lead.
The consensus agreement principle under strain
The United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) provides the
multilateral framework for COP, the forum where countries negotiate collective climate
decisions. The process is anchored by the view that all countries must act on climate change,

but the extent of their efforts depends on national circumstances. This puts the onus on
developed countries to lead. The question is whether this principle still holds against a more
fragmented backdrop and transactional diplomatic context as national budgets prioritize
security and business competitiveness. With the COP process still separating developed and
developing countries by their 1992 UNFCCC classifications, developed countries are continuing

to push for wealthier developing nations such as China and Saudi Arabia to play a greater role
on finance. This has been a major point of contention at recent summits.
The US stepping back has far-reaching implications
The US skipped COP30 and left the Paris Agreement. It will formally exit the UNFCCC in
February 2027. This retreat has weighed on broader climate-finance decisions, both from a
monetary sense and through the loss of negotiating power. While cities, states and businesses

from the US continue to support COP efforts (e.g., groups like America Is All In), the federal
administration’s absence has put more pressure on the EU and China to lead.
What does an evolving process look like?
Because formal COP decisions require consensus (or lack of objection), progress often stalls or
moves slowly. COP30 saw Brazil move items where there was strong, but not unanimous,
support such as the fossil fuel roadmap, to separate streams outside the formal COP process.

Countries disappointed with the lack of stronger mitigation action also launched a separate
summit on transitioning away from fossil fuels, held this past April in Colombia. While COP is
the flagship global forum, these actions demonstrate a shift towards “coalitions of the willing”
for issues where global agreement is not possible.
Sources: EU Council, EU Parliament, Carbon Brief, UN, WRI

TO P I C  7 | G EO P O L I TI C S  &  MU L TI L ATER AL I S M (2  o f 2 )
Much will rest on the approach of the EU and China

With the US absent, the EU and China are the two actors with the greatest diplomatic and economic leverage with which to broker compromise agreements between the different factions at
COP31. This will require careful diplomacy, with their interests not fully aligned in some areas; discussions around trade and climate finance may be particularly challenging. At the same time,
both have reiterated their continued support of climate action and multilateral decision making; a successful COP outcome would back up these commitments.

### Page 15

TO P I C  8 | AI  &  C L I MATE
The Antalya AI Pledge puts data-centre power demand on the agenda
We expect AI’s climate potential and rising electricity demand to make it a prominent COP31 topic, prompting debate over COPs’ role in data-centre energy use. The Antalya AI Pledge invites
governments and companies to commit voluntarily to sustainable infrastructure and environmental transparency. Its call for zero- and low-emissions energy infrastructure leaves room for

nuclear power, prompting discussion of its role in meeting data-centre demand alongside renewables.
The Antalya AI Pledge seeks to build stakeholder support for sustainable AI buildout
Türkiye is encouraging stakeholders (inc. corporates) to commit to the pledge, asking
signatories to “declare our shared ambition to make AI part of the climate solution. This
means sustainably powered, efficiently designed, transparently measured and available to all”.

Signatories will commit to: 1) Power and build AI infrastructure and hardware sustainably, 2)
Design, manage or procure AI responsibly across its lifecycle, 3) Support assessment and
reporting of the environmental impacts of AI systems, 4) Strengthen financial support and
technical infrastructure for AI for climate action.
The AI for Clean Technologies Initiative seeks to highlight AI climate solutions
The initiative plans to develop AI-enabled pilot projects (e.g. smart energy systems) and then

link the projects with industry partners and investors, aiming to demonstrate AI’s potential to
contribute to sustainability goals. For grids, the IEA sees AI improving forecasting, congestion
management and outage response, helping operators get more from existing lines.
The dialogue here will set the stage for AI discussions at future COPs
While the pledge is voluntary, many of these themes are already beginning to appear in early-
stage regulatory demands, with transparency and disclosure receiving particular focus. There

will be questions over how effective the pledge can be given the US has disengaged from the
COP process, though California could send a strong signal if it commits to the pledge. We will
be particularly focused on how the tech industry responds. Despite the trend of greenhushing,
we expect many to send delegations to Antalya, with AI companies and investors viewing
responsible practices and community engagement as key to avoiding backlash and delays to

AI buildout. Note that responsible AI was also a hot topic at the Principles for Responsible
Investment conference this year in October.
Sources: COP31 Presidency, IEA

### Page 16

TO P I C  9 | N ATU R E &  C AR B O N  MAR K ETS
Nature and carbon markets have key roles to play in supporting climate action
Carbon credit discussions will centre on government policies to boost demand and corporate engagement, with much of the formal UN architecture now in place. If carbon markets can scale,
the benefits would be wide-ranging (e.g. supporting emissions targets and providing finance for developing countries). Credibility and greenwashing concerns remain key risks however; we

expect prices to diverge in favour of high-integrity credits. The Tropical Forest Forever Facility (TFFF) now has ~$7.8bn of sponsor capital against a $10bn end-2026 target.
Sources: Tropical Forest Forever Facility, Coalition to Grow Carbon Markets
16
Voluntary carbon market development
Türkiye has stated a focus on carbon markets at COP31, particularly on government actions to
boost demand, highlighting the potential for carbon markets to unlock more than $50bn in

additional annual finance for developing nations. The Coalition to Grow Carbon Markets
(made up of 13 governments) has announced it will unveil a “Policy Playbook” at COP31 to
support market development. While much of Article 6 (carbon credits under UN process) has
moved to delivery, countries will aim to reach agreement on the funding model for Article 6.2
at COP31. Further carbon market progress stands to have multiple benefits; it can support
developed countries in hitting climate goals while reducing immediate decarbonisation

requirements, while also providing finance and supporting ecosystems in developing ones.
Understanding the pushback
Carbon markets have seen pushback over credit integrity (including regulatory scrutiny) and
the risk that offsets delay domestic cuts. UN involvement is needed to enforce common
accounting and integrity rules, such as the Article 6 rulebook finalised at COP29 and due for
review in 2028, that stop the same reduction being claimed twice. Without that backstop,

fragmented bilateral and voluntary schemes risk eroding the trust needed to scale the market.
Update on the TFFF
The Tropical Forest Forever Facility (TFFF), launched at COP30 last year, aims for $10bn of
sponsor contributions by year-end. It pays tropical-forest countries per hectare conserved,
discounts payments for each hectare lost or degraded, and directs at least 20% to Indigenous
Peoples and local communities. Its Charter was adopted on 22 July 2026 and received a

tailwind following the re-election of Brazilian President Lula, who is a key supporter. Shortly
after Lula’s re-election, the Netherlands pledged up to $500mn to the fund, bringing the total
pledged to $7.8bn. Further financing commitments are key, with Norway’s $3bn contribution
conditional on the fund reaching at least $10bn by the end of 2026.
Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Preview  |  October 2026

TO P I C  1 0 | P AC I F I C , O C EANS  & S EA L EV EL  R I S E
Oceans will be a priority item, with Australia promising to champion Pacific interests
Australia’s ask for ceding the hosting was a Pacific pre-COP, and it has promised to continue championing Pacific interests at COP31. The Taku Pakasoa Declaration, signed at the pre-COP,

reflects Pacific priorities, calling for faster emissions cuts, more adaptation finance and financial system reform. The climate-ocean nexus will also be a focus, including investable solutions.
Championing Pacific interests is a key focus for Australia
As part of the hosting compromise, Australia co-hosted the official pre-COP with Fiji and
Tuvalu (5–8 Oct), attended by countries including Germany, the UAE and India (though some
questioned the low number of international leaders who attended). Australia has pledged to

keep championing Pacific interests as President of the Negotiations.
Taku Pakasoa Declaration reflects Pacific Priorities
Signed on 6 Oct at the pre-COP, it calls for faster emissions cuts, a substantial increase in
climate finance (particularly for adaptation in capacity-constrained countries), reform of the
international financial architecture, and for elevating the ocean-climate nexus. It also
welcomes the 35% electrification goal.
The challenges of rising sea levels are gaining increasing prominence

Sea levels in the region are rising faster than the global average, and half of Pacific Island
infrastructure lies within 500 metres of the coast. The UN General Assembly adopted the UN
Declaration on Sea Level Rise by consensus on 24 Sept; global sea levels rose nearly 6mm in
2024, the highest annual increase on record.
Oceans are key to climate stability, but more finance is needed
Pacific nations emphasise the critical role of oceans as a natural environmental regulator.

They absorb 30% of all carbon dioxide emissions (largest carbon sink) and capture 90% of the
excess heat generated by these emissions. Investor interest in sustainable ocean solutions is
growing, with 2026 YTD blue bond issuance already an annual record, yet bond issuance
supporting UN SDG 14: Life Below Water trails many other categories; scaling this up will be a
focus at COP31. A commitment to delivering “tangible, bankable ocean outcomes” at COP31 is

one of 13 points included as part of the Trabzon Ocean Call to Action unveiled by Türkiye,
while the ORRAA has unveiled a Blue Climate Finance Pathway.
Sources: Taku Pakasoa Declaration (UNFCCC), UN Declaration on Sea Level Rise

Ethiopia will host COP32 in 2027, though questions over how COP will evolve are in focus
COP has shifted from setting targets to implementation. Ethiopia will host COP32 in 2027, where we expect a focus on delivering commitments and climate finance access, reflecting African
priorities. COP33 falls to Asia-Pacific in 2028, with China reportedly considering a bid and South Korea potentially interested. COP31–33 will test delivery in a fragmented world, with a second

Global Stocktake concluding in 2028. Its outcome should inform countries’ next national climate plans (NDCs, their emissions-reduction pledges), due in 2030.

AP P END I X  |   COP  TER MS
Appendix A: Key COP terms used in this report
This appendix explains the main COP process terms and negotiating concepts used throughout the report. Definitions are summarised for non-specialist readers and reflect how each term is
used in this deck.
Sources: UNFCCC; COP30 and COP31 Presidency documents; and the other sources referenced in this report
19
Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Preview  |  October 2026
TERM DEFINITION

Action Agenda Voluntary, non-binding pledges and initiatives launched alongside the formal talks. It is separate from negotiated decisions.
Annex II / Non-Annex I UNFCCC country classification. Annex II covers developed economies; Non-Annex I covers most other countries, including China and India.
Article 6 Paris Agreement framework for international carbon markets. Article 6.2 covers bilateral trading of carbon credits between countries.
Article 9

Paris Agreement finance provisions. Article 9.1 covers developed
countries’ obligation to provide finance. COP30 set a two-year work
programme for 2026-27.
Baku to Belém Roadmap
to $1.3T
Roadmap to scale climate finance to $1.3tn a year by 2035 from all
sources, inclusive of the $300bn NCQG.
Bonn sessions Mid-year technical talks of the UNFCCC Subsidiary Bodies (e.g. SB64) in Bonn, Germany, which prepare the ground for COP discussions.
Brazil fossil fuel roadmap COP30 Presidency’s voluntary roadmap for transitioning away from fossil fuels, pursued outside the formal UN negotiations.

Consensus How COP decisions are taken: adopted when no party formally objects, so a single party can theoretically block or dilute an outcome.
COP / COPs Conference of the Parties: the UNFCCC’s annual decision-making summit, where nearly 200 parties negotiate climate action.
COP Presidency The host country’s team that chairs the conference. For COP31, Türkiye’s Murat Kurum is COP President and leads the Action Agenda.
TERM DEFINITION
Final COP text Decisions adopted by consensus at the close of the COP, setting its formal outcome.

Formal agenda Negotiated items on the official COP agenda, decided by consensus. Separate from the voluntary Action Agenda.
Global Goal on
Adaptation (GGA)
Paris Agreement goal to strengthen resilience. COP30 adopted 59
indicators; the link to finance is contested.
Global Stocktake (GST) Five-yearly review of progress under the Paris Agreement. The second runs from COP31 to COP33 (2028).
Just transition
mechanism
Belém Action Mechanism agreed at COP30, focused on fairness in the

shift to a low-carbon economy.
Loss and damage fund Fund set up at COP27 and operationalised in 2023 to support vulnerable developing countries.
NCQG New Collective Quantified Goal (COP29): at least $300bn a year for developing countries by 2035, within a $1.3tn ambition.
NDC Nationally determined contribution: each country’s national climate plan, with emissions targets and actions, updated every five years.
Negotiating groups Blocs that coordinate national positions, such as the G77 & China, the EU, LDCs and AOSIS.

Paris Agreement 2015 treaty to hold warming well below 2°C and pursue 1.5°C, with national climate plans (NDCs) as its core mechanism.
Pre-COP Ministerial meeting ahead of the COP to test positions and build consensus. For COP31, Australia co-hosted a Pacific pre-COP.
President of Negotiations COP31 role held by Australia’s Chris Bowen, who drafts final texts and steers government-to-government talks.
UNFCCC UN Framework Convention on Climate Change: the 1992 treaty behind the climate process. Its parties meet annually at the COP.

Appendix C: The level of alarm over global warming has begun to move higher (again)
As part of our monthly Climate & Sustainability Survey, we ask consumers in the UK, US, Germany, France, Spain and Italy for their views on global warming. In Q3 2026, we recorded a notable
rebound in alarm regarding the pace of global warming, particularly in the UK and Europe following a summer of climate extremes. The level of alarm had begun to dip globally post the 2024 US

presidential election in November.

Document 364: Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower_

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower?
Date:    1 hour ago
Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Could Midterms Give Wall Street
A Cold Shower?
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 2 Nov 2026
View in browser

I. When Gridlock Isn't Good for Stocks
The US midterm elections will be over tomorrow night. According to
betting markets, Democrats have roughly a 95% probability of winning the
House and a 76% probability of taking the Senate (charts).
We suspect that stock investors have yet to fully discount the possibility of
a Democratic sweep of Congress. Some may remain skeptical that polls
confirming strong support for Democrats are accurate. They might reckon

### Page 2

that Democrats are more likely than Republicans to talk to pollsters over
the phone.
In any event, Wall Street traditionally welcomes political gridlock because
it reduces the likelihood that Congress will pass extremist legislation. But
this time could be different for two reasons.
First, deepening partisanship could turn gridlock into damaging political
paralysis at a time when the Bond Vigilantes are already uneasy about
Washington's unsustainable fiscal trajectory. A Democratic sweep could

complicate negotiations over government spending and the debt ceiling.
Prolonged shutdowns and fiscal standoffs could push Treasury yields
higher, weighing on equity valuations.

### Page 3

Second, gridlock's traditional ability to constrain policymaking may not
hold this time. President Donald Trump has relied heavily on executive
authority, with some of his most market-disruptive policies, including tariffs
and military action against Iran, pursued largely without congressional
approval. A Democratic-controlled Congress may therefore do little to
restrain him. Moreover, with the midterms behind him, Trump may feel
even less constrained politically and more willing to pursue controversial

policies through executive action. Hence, a blue wave could deliver a cold
shower for stocks, though we would view a significant selloff as a buying
opportunity given the remarkable strength of the economy and corporate
earnings.
One encouraging development ahead of the elections was today's reports
that the US and Iran are working on a peaceful diplomatic solution to their
war. Iranian President Masoud Pezeshkian said he has granted his
government greater authority to pursue peace talks, while President

Trump and Vice President Vance also signaled progress. Hopes for a deal
sent the price of Brent crude oil down 7.6% to $104.77 per barrel (chart).
However, we have been here before with apparent diplomatic
breakthroughs. We still see a high risk of military re-escalation. The bid-
ask spread remains wide, with Tehran demanding sanctions relief, the
release of frozen assets, and an end to the US naval blockade of Iranian
ports, while Washington insists on nuclear concessions. Meanwhile, Iran's

leverage over the Strait of Hormuz has been weakening. According to
shipping data, for roughly half of September, Gulf oil exporters shipped
more crude than they were shipping before the war.

### Page 4

II. US Economy Continues To Roar
The US economy remains strong despite the volatility of the domestic
political and geopolitical scenes. Consider the following:
(1) Construction spending. Construction spending fell 1.9% m/m in
September to $2.19 trillion (saar), reversing most of August's upwardly
revised 2.0% increase. Nevertheless, spending remains just below last
year's record high (chart).
September's decline was driven by a 5.5% m/m drop in private residential

construction, attributable to a 13.0% plunge in home improvements
(chart). Elevated materials costs and higher mortgage rates continue to
weigh on the housing sector. Meanwhile, private nonresidential
construction rose 1.1% m/m, supported by continued investment in data
centers and power infrastructure.

### Page 5

The AI investment boom remains a powerful tailwind. Data center
construction spending surged 8.6% m/m and 90% y/y in September,
reaching a record $96.5 billion annual rate (chart).
(2) Manufacturing. The ISM M-PMI rose from 54.5 in September to 55.5
in October, marking its tenth consecutive month of expansion, the longest
streak since 2022. Production jumped to the highest reading since
November 2021, while new orders edged up to 55.5. Employment also

remained in expansionary territory (chart).

### Page 6

Inflationary pressures remain troublesome. The prices-paid index eased
from 77.9 to 75.2 in October but remained above 70 for the ninth
consecutive month. Regional Fed surveys also point to persistent price
pressures. Higher energy costs, tariffs, and elevated metals prices
continue to boost input costs (chart).
(3) Bank lending. The Fed's latest Senior Loan Officer Opinion Survey
(SLOOS) painted a mixed picture of credit demand. Banks reported
weaker demand for commercial and industrial (C&I) loans, mortgages,

and auto loans in Q4, while credit-card demand strengthened (chart).

### Page 7

Lending standards remained broadly unchanged for C&I loans but
tightened somewhat for commercial real estate loans (chart).
Encouragingly, actual bank lending tells a happier story. C&I loans surged
11.3% y/y through October 21, while total loans and leases increased
7.9% y/y (chart).

### Page 8

Credit growth is broad across bank sizes, with lending up 8.1% y/y at
large banks and 5.4% at small banks (chart).
Meanwhile, banks' allowances for loan and lease losses have remained
relatively stable at around $202 billion, despite the acceleration in lending
(chart).

### Page 9

clause.

Document 397: Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer

Source PDF: all report pdf/Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer
Date:    6 hours ago
Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 4 Nov 2026
View in browser

I. Why Wall Street Is Cheering the Blue Sweep
Ahead of Tuesday's midterm elections, we warned that a Democratic
sweep of Congress could deliver a cold shower to Wall Street. It still could,
but it didn't do so today. Democrats captured both chambers of Congress,
flipping seven Republican-held Senate seats, including historic victories in
Texas, Kansas, and South Carolina. Financial markets put a positive spin
on the blue wave.
Oil prices fell on hopes that the US and Iran are more likely now to resolve
their war diplomatically. If so, then the outlook for inflation will improve. So
the Fed might not have to raise the federal funds rate as much. The

### Page 2

federal deficit might not widen further if Democrats refuse to increase
defense spending, while President Donald Trump vetoes their attempts to
provide Medicare for all. If Democrats slow the AI buildout, hyperscalers'
profits and free cash flow will increase. Fewer AI-related bond issues
would also help keep a lid on bond yields.
Initially, Nasdaq-100 futures plunged as much as 2.4% overnight, while
S&P 500 futures fell 1.4%. But the selloff reversed after Trump struck a

surprisingly conciliatory tone, congratulating Democrats and saying he
was willing to work with them.
That is when the cheer on Wall Street started. The Dow surged 3.0%
today, the strongest post-midterm rally since 1982. The S&P 500 jumped
1.6%, its best session in three months. The Nasdaq-100 advanced 0.9%.
The relief rally extended beyond equities. The 10-year Treasury yield fell
to 4.90%, back in what we have called the "old normal" range of
4.00%-5.00% (chart).

The dollar index (DXY) fell more than 1.5%, its biggest decline since
September. Gold rallied back above $4,000 an ounce. It might be finally
bottoming.

### Page 3

de-escalation in the Middle East
Brent crude plunged 8.7% to $94.62 a barrel amid hopes of Middle East
de-escalation. The nearby futures price of gasoline plunged to $2.73
(chart).
Investors may be warming up to the idea that some constraints on the AI
buildout could be healthy, as we have been arguing recently. Democratic
victories in key data center states, including Texas and Georgia, raise the
prospect of tighter construction restrictions amid concerns about electricity

and water consumption.
Hyperscalers rallied today, with Amazon and Microsoft gaining more than
6%, as capital discipline could ease concerns about AI investment returns
and improve free cash flow (chart). AI infrastructure providers struggled,

### Page 4

however. The PHLX Semiconductor Index fell 3.1% amid fears of weaker
demand from a slower buildout. We may be entering an AI Goldilocks
zone, with enough restraint to take some air out of the bubble without
derailing the boom.
II. The US Economy Continues To Perform Well
The economy and, therefore, earnings remain fundamentally resilient,
which continues to support the bull market. With the midterms behind us
and political uncertainty receding, investors can refocus on these strong

fundamentals. Here are the highlights of today's upbeat economic
indicators:
(1) ISM Services PMI. The ISM-NM PMI edged down modestly to 54.5 in
October, marking its 28th consecutive month of expansion (chart).
Underlying demand remained solid, with new orders and business activity
both comfortably above the 50.0 expansion threshold. Encouragingly, the
employment index rose from 50.1 to 51.4, marking its second consecutive
month of expansion.

### Page 5

Inflation is still a concern. The ISM prices-paid indexes remained elevated
in October (chart).
(2) Labor market. The average of the ISM employment indexes rose to
51.9 in October, its highest reading in roughly three years (chart). ADP
reported an 86,000 increase in private payrolls in October, beating
consensus expectations of 67,000.

### Page 6

(3) Trade balance. The latest trade data point to resilient US and global
economic growth. Merchandise imports rose 2.4% m/m in September to a
record $3.44 trillion annualized, while exports increased 2.5% m/m to
$2.09 trillion. The merchandise trade deficit widened to $1.35 trillion
annualized (chart).
Nominal and real US merchandise exports plus imports rose 21.1% y/y
and 10.1%, respectively, in September (chart). That suggests global trade
remains firmly in expansion territory and is consistent with strong global

economic growth.

### Page 7

Selected technology imports reached a record $61.3 billion in September,
reflecting the AI buildout’s heavy reliance on imported semiconductors,
servers, and other hardware (chart).

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

People Bank of China (October 20)

We expect the PBOC to keep the 1Y LPR 
unchanged next week . Industrial 
production is expected to edge up from 
5.2% yoy in August to 5.3% in September, 
supported by firm external demand. 
Exports stayed strong, rising 36.3% yoy 
in September, driven by the regional AI -
electronics upcycle.

Domestic momentum, however, remains 
uneven. Retail sales growth is expected 
to moderate to 0.3% yoy  in September. 
Household sentiment is weak amid 
uncertain job prospects, slower income 
growth and elevated precautionary 
savings, while subdued property prices 
continue to weigh on household wealth 
and consumption. Investment also stays 
weak, with fixed as set investment 
expected to contract further by 7.5% yoy 
ytd in September. Corporate investment 
is cautious amid the anti -involution 
campaign and geopolitical tensions, 
while the property sector continues to be 
a major drag.

Policymakers are likely to maintain a 
measured policy stance, relying on 
targeted fiscal support to sustain growth 
rather than further rate cuts.

Bank Indonesia and Bangko Sentral ng 
Pilipinas (Oct 21 and 22).

Bank Indonesia is expected to extend its 
pause, as they lean on non-rate measures, 
including intervention and hedging 
facilities to help the currency.  On Friday, 
MSCI signaled a  potential shift in 
Indonesia’s status to a standalone 
status, rather than a reclassification to 
frontier market , after consultations are 
complete in mid -2027. Any ensuing risk -
off move in the currency , would likely 
prompt greater central bank vigilance 
and intervention.

Our base case for the benchmark rate to 
be held unchanged this quarter , backed 
by a lower conviction around back -to-
back US rate hikes in Oct and relative 
rupiah stability. Separately, the 
government announced additional 
support for low-income households in 4Q 
by way of in -kind assistance and wage 
subsidies. This year’s fiscal deficit i s 
likely to close in at -2.85% of GDP, close 
to our forecast at -2.9%, but few 
uncertainties facing the 2027 math (note).

We expect a 25bp hike from the BSP to 
5.25% in October , after three rate 
increases earlier in the year. Rising by the 
highest pace in Asia, Philippines inflation 
surged to 7.2% y/y in September from 6.1% 
in August, matching this year's peak and 
approaching a three -year high.  Inflation 
risks remain tilted to the upside, driven by 
further public transport fare adjustments, 
elevated oil prices, recent wage hikes 
and peso depreciation. Fiscal space is, 
meanwhile, limited to support 
households, via subsidies and/ instate 
price caps. Governor Remolona also 
signalled that the BSP remains ready to 
act on rates to contain inflation. With 
inflationary pressures likely to be 
persistent, the likelihood of further hikes 
in 4Q26 rises.

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Bank of Korea (October 22)

The Bank of Korea is expected to hold its 
policy rate at 3.00%, following back -to-
back rate hikes in July and August. AI -
driven growth momentum remains strong, 
as reflected in September export growth 
of around 80% YoY. However, the 
urgency for further tightening has eased, 
with CPI inflation stabilising at 2.9% YoY 
in September. The recent KOSPI 
correction and continued KRW strength 
also reduce the need for another near -
term hike. We maintain our call for a final 
25bp rate hike in November, bringing the 
policy rate to 3.25% by year-end.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Japan

September trade and CPI data are due 
shortly. Export growth is expected to 
remain solid at 24% YoY, supported by 
continued strength in AI -related 
semiconductor exports. CPI inflation is 
projected to rise notably to 2.6% YoY, 
driven by the phase -out of publ ic utility 
subsidies, higher global energy prices, 
and persistent inflationary pressures 
from a weak yen. The BOJ is likely to 
increasingly acknowledge that its 2% 
inflation target is within reach. However, 
it is expected to keep rates unchanged 
at 1.25% at its October 29–30 meeting to 
assess the impact of the latest rate hike 
in September.

Taiwan

September export orders and industrial 
production data are due shortly. Export 
orders are expected to sustain 60% YoY 
growth, while industrial production is 
projected to expand by 20% YoY. The 
global AI boom faces growing headwinds 
from hyperscalers’ cash flow pressures, 
central bank rate hikes, and higher bond 
yields. However, our high -frequency 
indicators across AI demand (token 
expenditure), investment (hyperscaler  
capex and data centre construction 
spending), and supply (semiconductor 
sales and exports) show no clear signs of 
a significant slowdown so far.

Malaysia

Malaysia’s external demand likely 
remained strong in September 2026, 
while inflation edged up slightly. We 
expect export growth to sustain its 
robust expansion at 45% yoy , with 
momentum anchored by strong demand 
for electrical and electronics goods. Oil 
& gas shipments likely remained buoyant. 
However, high base effects prevented a 
faster pace of expansion.

We expect headline inflation to rise 
modestly to 2.1% yoy in September 2026. 
The renewed increase in global oil prices 
likely contributed to higher increases in 
domestic energy costs, although fuel 
subsidies continued to limit the cost 
pass-through and keep underlying 
inflationary pressures contained. Food 
inflation likely also firmed, with adverse 
weather conditions associated with El 
Nino posing upside risks.

### Page 4

The Week Ahead October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         4

Singapore

We expect Singapore’s core and 
headline inflation to edge up slightly to 
2.3% yoy and 2.4% yoy , respectively, in 
September 2026, following modest 
increases to 2.2% yoy and 2.3% yoy in 
August. Inflationary pressures likely 
reflected firm private transport price 
increases amid higher petrol prices 
despite slower car price growth , the 
ongoing pass -through of higher global 
energy prices to fuel -exposed services 
items, positive contribution from 
elevated electricity & gas prices, firmer 
food inflation, and a slight increase in 
still-muted accommodation inflation. 
Firm, albeit not acc elerating, inflation in 
September 2026, which may be close to 
its peak in the Monetary Authority of 
Singapore’s assessment, likely validated 
its decision to maintain SGD NEER policy 
settings at the October 2026 review, 
following two proactive tightening 
moves in April and July.

Hong Kong

CPI is projected to edge up from 1.7% yoy 
in August to 1.9% in September, 
supported by improving consumption 
sentiment and higher global energy 
prices. Mainland visitor arrivals 
increased by 29.0% yoy in September on 
a daily average basis, providing furthe r 
support to retail spending and 
consumer-related services. Meanwhile, 
renewed conflict in the Middle East 
during the month has pushed up global 
energy prices, adding to upward 
pressure on utility and transport costs.

《地緣風險變數使油價短期仍偏上》
OPEC+決議11月產出配額維持 9月規模，預期目前
配額將持續至今年年底 ，下一輪恢復最後一套 200
萬桶/日的額外減產措施將在明年視市況調整。依市
場推估，中東區域海運原油及成品油運輸出口量9月
底曾回至接近九成 ，受荷姆茲海峽油輪襲擊事件增
加，伊朗革命衛隊預告管理範圍擴大 ，使原油運輸
開始下降 ，但曼德海峽因沙國駐葉門大使稱已清除
胡塞武裝勢力 ，預期曼德海峽運輸量將不受影響 。
但受轉運延長運輸時間 、避開受制裁的原油採購 ，
加上搶補低庫存 ，市場追逐現貨使布油呈現大幅現

貨溢價，七國集團(G7)雖計畫在未來四個月內釋放
總計原油與柴油戰略儲備各 0.5億桶，總計1億桶，
但預計佔每天原油用量低 ，僅短期壓抑油價 ，主要
重點仍在美伊談判、美國向中東增兵、伊朗襲船，

《天然氣價格：美持平或略低於去年 ，歐洲偏上行 》
美國鑽井活動緩升 ，增加較多在天然氣 ，隨美國新
的液化天然氣出口工廠上線 ，逐漸改善管道瓶頸 ，
天然氣 10月庫存僅高於五年均值 2%，注入期可持
續增至11月中，預估今年冬天暖氣度日數將持平去
年 ， 並 比 過 去 五 個 冬 季 的 均 值 低1% ， 預估美國
4Q26天然氣價格將因季節性走升，但持平或略低於
去年。卡達能源LNG船已出口 ，但非往歐洲 ，歐洲
儲氣量雖拉升 ，但僅達 70%，遠低於過往 ，TTF價
格須優於亞洲以吸引運往亞洲的 LNG轉往歐洲 ，加

上中東地緣緊張，預期4Q26 TTF價格仍有上行風險。
重點摘要
• 天然氣：美國天然氣出口增
加，庫存增速下降 ，使4Q26
美國天然氣價格持平或略低
於去年同期；歐洲因庫存偏
低的回補需求將使天然氣價
格偏上行。
• 釋儲及美俄供應柴油協議雖
對全球柴油供給影響有限 ，
但超額柴油利潤恐見頂。
• 預期綜合油企 、煉油廠雖因
汽油淡季使 4Q26 獲 利 低 於
3Q26，但獲利年比仍大幅成
長，維持股東回報 ，較看好
綜合油企。
下圖/預期柴油價格仍暫時維持高檔後緩降
資料來源：EIA，2026/10/6，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/10/8
2
《釋儲及放寬制裁增加柴油恐使煉油利潤見頂》
受歐美老舊煉油廠關廠 ，煉油產能因俄烏 、中東戰
事受損，估計全球煉油廠開工率年減近 800萬桶/日，
加上中、俄禁止柴油出口 ，使柴油供應比原油更緊
張。美國柴油庫存低於五年區間下緣 ，柴油價格創
高，因美未禁止柴油出口 ，柴油利潤保持強勁 。但
美協調G7國家釋出柴油儲備為期四個月 ，達成美俄

柴油供應協議增加供給 ，雖粗略換算為短期每日供
給增加不到全球 1%，預期對柴油價格壓力影響僅
5%內，明年隨設備漸修復、地緣風險降低後，才緩
降，但美國超額柴油利潤恐見頂。
《4Q26能源類股較看好綜合油企》
預估美綜合油企 4Q26受惠油價高於預期 、LNG出
口增加，柴油高裂解價差 ，但進入汽油需求淡季 ，
獲利季比下滑 ，但年比仍大幅成長 ，現金流仍強大 ，
維持資本紀律及股東回報 。雖成品油供應仍較原油
吃緊，但煉油商超額利潤恐見頂 ，較看好綜合油企 。

《預期金價短期不利因素多，但中期緩升》
近月金價歷經 美國通膨及經濟成長 ，美國FED採取
預防管理式升息一碼 ，認為年底前仍有升息一碼空
間，但受就業降溫及通膨低於預期 ，市場降低10月
升息預期 ，加上法國國債被拋售蔓延全球債市使美
長期公債殖利率及美元走強 、黃金淨多頭部位下降 ，
壓低本月金價下跌 ，也跌破季線 。預期短線金價受
債券殖利率飆升 、實質利率走升 及美元走強 相對不
利金價走勢 ，震盪放大。但若美債收益率回落 、擔
憂債務可持續性 、分散交易對手風險及多元化資產
配置，中國人行連續23個月購金，並加大購金步伐 ，

美元有撐，維持金價中期緩步上行。

Document 592: 中金：美股跌到位了么？

Source PDF: all report pdf/中金：美股跌到位了么？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

中⾦认为，虽然纳斯达克、费半估值已回落⾄历史 44% 和 14% 分位数，但市场缺乏关键催
化： AI 收⼊落后于投资，五⼤云⼚商⾃由现⾦流转负⾄ -318 亿美元，边际 ROIC 降⾄
6.1% ；传统需求⼜受⾼利率压制。若 AI 额外盈利增⻓被抹去，标普 500 仍有约 10% 下⾏空
间。明年⼀季度是关键节点 —— 届时美联储转向、四季报兑现、新国会就任将同步验证。
摘要：
美股近期波动明显放⼤，中期选举后的政策 “ 噪⾳ ” 诱发了市场动荡（《⺠主党 “ 横扫 ” 的资产含
义》），关键也是 AI ⾃⾝当前⾯临需求 “ ⻘⻩不接 ” 、投资⾼企等诸多 “ 问题 ” ，被提前暴露《如何看
待美股⼤跌？》）。
当然，这种快速的出清对⻓期⾛势⽽⾔或不是坏事，有助于消化过⾼的预期和仓位，这对未来
在新催化出现下重拾上⾏反⽽有利，只不过需要等待新的催化出现。要知道，今年以来的美股
涨幅基本都是盈利贡献的，⽽⾮依靠估值扩张，经过近期回调后，估值更是快速⾛低。除道琼
斯 19.6 倍估值仍在过去 20 年历史分位数的 86% ，标普 500 、纳斯达克估值分别回落⾄ 17.8 倍和

20.3 倍，对应 70% 和 44% 过去 20 年分位数，费半甚⾄降⾄ 13.8 倍，仅为 14% 的过去 20 年分位
数。
图表 1 ：标普 500 、纳斯达克等估值均已明显回落，费半甚⾄降⾄ 14% 历史分位数
资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部
今年美国基本⾯的 “K 型分化 ” 使得市场集中并依赖少数⽅向： 1 ）美国 Mag7 贡献了 2026 年以来
标普 500 涨幅的 22% ， 10 ⽉中旬下跌前⼀度贡献 40% ；2 ）五⼤云⼚商 2026 年前三季度盈利占标
普 500 指数的 20% ，贡献了标普 500 盈利增⻓的 25% ；3 ）⻰头集中效应也依然存在，年初以来标
普 500 上涨 8% ，但在 200 ⽇均线的股票⽐例却由年初的 60% 下降⾄ 55% ， 8-9 ⽉背离尤为明显。
然⽽，当前 AI 缺乏模型和应⽤层⾯催化、还遇上了政策不确定性，传统需求则受⾼利率压制。换
⾔之，主⼒⽅向⾯临挑战，传统⽅向尚待接棒，解释了整体的动荡。
图表 2 ：美国 Mag7 贡献了 2026 以来标普 500 涨跌幅的 22%

资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部

### Page 3

所以，市场需要两个催化剂：要么 AI 出现巨⼤突破，要么美联储结束紧缩甚⾄转向宽松。在此
之前，我们建议谨慎观望：1 ）关注短期是否有流动性负反馈，或进⼀步政策扰动（新国会明年
初到任前的博弈）； 2 ）控制仓位，为后续配置保留空间； 3 ）调整结构，即便仍在科技内部，也
可以优先关注 AI 敞⼝低、或者估值低且更有议价能⼒的云⼚商和计算芯⽚等。
明年⼀季度是关键点： 1 ）美联储明显转向的契机预计要到明年初。10 ⽉强劲的⾮农强化了 12 ⽉
加息概率，明年初通胀在⾼基数带动下回落更确定；2 ）明年 1 ⽉披露的四季报。科技企业的
ROIC 可能继续承压，届时也更需要 AI 兑现新进展；3 ）美国新国会明年 1 ⽉ 3 ⽇就任。特朗普在
新国会到任前的政策与新当选议员不断发⾔可能会有持续扰动，市场需要消化，新国会就任后
可能逐步脱敏。所以，到那个时候，要不就是得 AI 有进展，要么就是得美联储紧缩预期退坡，
或许提供⽀撑或契机。
我们在《⺠主党 “ 横扫 ” 的资产含义》和《如何看待美股⼤跌？》中分别梳理了中期选举政策以及

市场动荡后的流动性扰动。本⽂中，我们进⼀步结合已经基本披露完毕的美股三季度业绩，从
基本⾯⻆度回答美股前景问题。
市场驱动⼒：盈利是市场的主导， AI 贡献六成增⻓；增速出现拐点
⾸先，盈利的强劲增⻓是美股的主要动⼒。年初⾄今，美股的主要指数涨幅均由盈利贡献，标
普 500 、道琼斯和纳斯达克指数分别上涨 8% 、 7% 和 6% ，其中动态盈利分别增⻓ 29% 、 17% 和
40% ，抵消了估值的收缩。三季度标普 500 盈利增⻓ 25% ，相⽐⼀季度和⼆季度的 32% 和 56% 有
所回落，但依然⾼于 2023-2025 年期间 12% 的季度盈利增速均值。
图表 5 ：今年以来的美股涨幅基本都是盈利贡献的，⽽⾮依靠估值扩张
资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部
其次， AI 相关板块贡献了六成的盈利增⻓。以通讯服务、信息技术和亚⻢逊估算 AI 板块的盈利情
况，三季度贡献了整体盈利增⻓的 62% ，低于⼆季度的 70% 。其中，信息技术贡献达 58% ，⽀
撑 AI 板块中的绝⼤部分增⻓，通讯服务则因⼦⾏业媒体娱乐增速⼤幅放缓，贡献明显⾛弱。这也

使得整体盈利增⻓系于 AI ⼀⾝，如果剔除 AI 板块的话，三季度盈利增速或将回落⾄ 15%。
图表 6 ：信息科技贡献本季度盈利增⻓的 58% ，通讯服务的贡献则由⼆季度的 24% 降⾄ 4%

### Page 4

资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部
图表 7 ： AI 相关板块贡献美国标普 500 整体盈利的六成
资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部
传统需求三季度盈利并不差，但⾼利率抑制下依然拖累盈利。从增速看，能源（ 150% ）、半导
体设备（ 110% ）、房地产管理与开发（ 49% ）、资本品（ 48% ）等板块领先，交运（ -37% ）、电
信服务（ -34% ）、⾦融服务（ -14% ）和保险（ -11% ）相对落后。从贡献来看，⼯业、原材料、
公⽤事业、必需消费等等板块合计贡献盈利的 10% ，⾦融和地产贡献则均转负，拖累整体增⻓
的 7.5% 。
第三，增速出现拐点， 2Q25 以来⾸次降速。三季度标普 500 盈利增⻓ 25% ，较今年前两个季度
放缓。市场对业绩更加 “ 挑剔 ” ，不仅要求盈利维持⾼增速，还需要不断超预期加速，也加重了三
季度财报超预期的难度。标普 500 指数成分股中超预期⽐例和幅度均回落，⽐例由⼆季度的 86%
⼩幅回落⾄ 84% ，超预期幅度则从⼆季度的 27% 显著回落到 3.5% ，为 2022 年四季度以来的最低

⽔平。其中，⾕歌和 Meta 所在通讯服务下降幅度最⼤，盈利由⼆季度超预期 99% 转为三季度低
于预期 19% 。
图表 8 ：标普 500 超预期⽐例从⼆季度的 86% ⼩幅回落⾄ 84% ，超预期幅度显著回落到 3.5%

### Page 5

资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部
AI ⽅向：收⼊落后投资；回报⾛低，但仍⾼于成本；能承受利率降⾄
6~6.5%
1) 收⼊落后于投资。资本开⽀加速扩张，⾃由现⾦流转负，外部融资压⼒上升。五⼤云⼚商三
季度合计资本开⽀规模由 1815 亿美元升⾄ 2300 亿美元，同⽐增速由⼆季度的 87% 加速⾄
118% 。但经营性现⾦流增速却明显回落，由⼆季度的 38% 下降⾄三季度的 19% ，五⼤云⼚商合
计 1980 亿美元，已不⾜以覆盖同期资本开⽀。这使得五⼤云⼚商⾃由现⾦流由⼆季度的 48 亿美
元转负⾄ -318 亿美元，除微软外，亚⻢逊、 Meta 、⾕歌和甲⻣⽂的三季度⾃由现⾦流均转负，
进⼀步推升外部融资需求。在 AI 需求尚未突破现有应⽤场景 “ 天花板 ” 、投资回报前景仍不明朗的
背景下，市场对融资成本和资⾦来源的关注明显上升，信⽤⻛险担忧随之加剧，五⼤云⼚商
CDS 纷纷升⾄历史⾼位。
图表 9 ：五⼤云⼚商合计⾃由现⾦流由⼆季度的 48 亿美元转负⾄ -318 亿美元

资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部
图表 10 ：五⼤云⼚商 CDS 也纷纷升⾄历史⾼位

### Page 6

资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部
2) 回报⾼于成本。边际 ROIC 进⼀步⾛低，但整体回报依然可以覆盖融资成本。伴随投⼊资本持
续扩张，新增经营回报未能同步，五⼤云⼚商边际 ROIC 由 2024 年⼆季度的⾼点 44% 持续下降⾄
6.1% ，带动整体 ROIC 进⼀步回落⾄ 15% 。不过，整体 ROIC 仍⾼于五⼤云⼚商当前约 10% 的加
权平均资本成本（ WACC ），意味着云⼚商依然可以获得超额回报。
图表 11 ：五⼤云⼚商整体 ROIC 由⼆季度的 16% 进⼀步回落⾄ 15% ，边际回报率也下滑⾄ 6.1%
资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部
边际回报下降使得 AI 能承受的利率⽔平下降⾄ 6~6.5% 。此前，我们测算如果五⼤云⼚商维持
ROIC 不变，假设 ROIC 与 WACC 之差收窄⾄ 2015-2023 年 AI 趋势兴起之前的平均⽔平 8ppt ，对应
可以承受的⽆⻛险利率为 7~7.5% （《 AI 从传统需求的 “ 朋友 ” 到 “ 敌⼈ ” ？》）。但随着三季度边际投

资回报进⼀步⾛弱， ROIC 与 WACC 之差已经收敛⾄历史均值，结合 2027 年的回报预期，AI 可承
受的⽆⻛险利率⽔平降⾄ 6~6.5%。
图表 12 ：结合 2027 年的回报预期， AI 可承受的⽆⻛险利率⽔平降⾄ 6~6.5%

### Page 7

资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部
3) 云⼚商 “ 重资产化 ” 对资本周转率形成压⼒。进⼀步拆分 ROIC 为税后经营利润率
（ NOPAT/Revenue ）和投⼊资本周转率（ Revenue/IC ），五⼤云⼚商税后经营利润率⾃ 2023 年
以来持续抬升，由 14.8% 升⾄三季度的 22.1% ；但资本周转率却持续回落，由 2023 年⼀季度
109% 的⾼点下降⾄三季度的 66% ，创 2015 年以来的新低。同时，⾃ 2025 年⼆季度以来，利润
率的改善放缓⽆法抵消资本周转率的拖累，这也导致五⼤云⼚商的 ROIC 持续下⾏。
图表 13 ：从 2025 年⼆季度以来，利润率的改善⽆法抵消资本周转率的负向拖累
资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部
科技内部进⼀步分化，部分硬件环节仍需消化增⻓预期，云⼚商定价相对合理。部分计⼊较⾼
增⻓预期的硬件基础设施，如⽹络互联 / 光模块、半导体制造、液冷 / 温控等近期估值明显回调，
例如半导体制造板块估值由 10 ⽉初的 90% 分位数快速下降⾄ 20% 左右。相⽐之下，云⼚商并未

给予新增盈利明显的估值溢价，估值调整空间也相对有限。
传统板块：利率敏感环节受⾼利率压制， AI 的挤压也逐步体现
传统需求尚未形成全⾯改善。消费⽅⾯，服务消费强于商品消费。消费者服务、⻝品饮料及烟
草、汽⻋与零部件盈利分别增⻓ 12% 、 8.7% 和 7.3% ，但耐⽤品与服装、家庭及个⼈⽤品分别下
降 5.4% 和 10.3% 。⼯业板块分化更明显，资本品盈利增⻓ 48.3% ，商业及专业服务增⻓ 16.2% ，

### Page 8

但受⾼油价成本侵蚀的交运则下降 37.1% 。⾦融板块中，银⾏盈利在净利息收⼊⽀撑下⼩幅增⻓
4.8% ，保险和⾦融服务则受投资损益波动等因素分别下降 10.7% 和 13.6% 。
增⻓仍有韧性，但利率敏感的增⻓环节依然持续磨底。近期公布的宏观数据反映当前美国增⻓
依然有韧性，三季度实际 GDP 环⽐年化增⻓ 3.4% ，⾼于预期的 3.2% 和⼆季度的 2.2% 。除 AI 相
关投资外，私⼈消费同样很强，尤其是医疗、餐饮和住宿等服务消费。 10 ⽉ ISM 制造业和⾮农也
⼤超预期，失业率也由 4.2% 降⾄ 4.1% 。但⾼利率持续抑制居⺠购房及信贷需求释放， 30 年期按
揭利率年初以来持续抬升， 10 ⽉⼀度升⾄ 7.4% 。地产销售依然低位徘徊， 9 ⽉成屋销售虽环⽐
⼩幅回升，但年化销量仅为 399 万套，新屋销售也降⾄ 67.6 万套。另⼀⽅⾯，回报⾛弱的 AI 也有
可能开始 “ 挤占 ” 传统需求的实物和⾦融要素资源。正如我们在《 AI 从传统需求的 “ 朋友 ” 到 “ 敌⼈ ” 》

中强调，此前 AI 需求带来的正外部性⽀撑了美国经济增⻓，但随着 AI 需要更多外部资⾦来⽀持投
资，对传统需求的挤出效应也是难以避免的， AI ⼤量发债和美联储依然需要加息就是典型例⼦。
图表 14 ：利率敏感的环节如房屋销售则⾃ 2023 年以来持续磨底
资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部
更⼤幅度的修复依然需要利率回落来配合。我们此前以地产和⾮ AI 企业测算，传统需求能够承受
的 10 年美债利率约为 4% 。⽬前虽有韧性，但⾼利率仍在持续挤压居⺠购房和中⼩企业信贷需求
的进⼀步释放，体现为居⺠部⻔的信⽤扩张动⼒不⾜。短期内，增⻓韧性和就业数据使得美联
储缺乏⽴即转向的动⼒，但传统需求的疲弱也不⽀持美联储更⼤幅度的加息。如果明年⼀季度
AI 预期仍⾛弱，那么市场对美联储的紧缩预期有望退坡，为利率下⾏和传统需求的修复创造条
件。
前景判断：市场需要紧缩结束或 AI 进展的催化，明年初是关键节点
⽬前市场对 AI 预期本⾝就处于 “ ⻘⻩不接 ” 的阶段，关键的问题在于没有需求和模型的新叙事和新

催化，所以也会放⼤信⽤和监管压⼒的⻛险。⾼强度资本开⽀不断挤压云⼚商⾃由现⾦流，导
致 AI 收⼊兑现速度和投资回报率的担忧不断升温，这与去年底 AI 进展不⾜时市场担忧私募股权和
信贷⻛险的逻辑类似。
这⼀背景下，市场需要两个催化剂：要么 AI 出现巨⼤突破，要么美联储结束紧缩甚⾄转向宽
松。如果都没出现的话，可能会影响投资者重新⼊场抄底的意愿，也会放⼤波动。短期需要关
注是否有流动性负反馈，或者进⼀步政策扰动（新国会明年初到任前，特朗普是否会利⽤这⼀
窗⼝做政策博弈）。明年⼀季度是关键验证点，国会到任、业绩期 AI 兑现、通胀回落都会出现变
化。

### Page 9

在此之前，我们短期依然建议谨慎观望，在上述催化出现前，短期关注是否会出现流动性的负
反馈以及政策进⼀步扰动。⽬前标普 500 指数已经回到年线附近，前期调整已经释放部分情绪，
但市场仍缺乏新的上⾏催化，流动性分项和 12 ⽉美联储加息等因素也可能带来进⼀步扰动。因
此，我们建议等待上述催化出现、投资回报前景更为清晰后，再进⾏配置。从极端情形看，若 AI
预期带来的额外盈利增⻓全部被 “ 抹去 ” ，动态盈利增速或回落⾄ 10% ，假设估值同步回落⾄ 16 〜
17 倍，则对应标普 500 指数仍有约 10% 的下跌空间。
除了控制好仓位外，也可以调整持仓结构，即便在科技内部也可以优先关注 AI 敞⼝低、或者估
值低且更有议价能⼒的云⼚商和计算芯⽚等。近期市场表现也反映了这⼀差异，此前增⻓预期
计⼊较为充分的环节回调更为明显，云⼚商则相对抗跌。以过去 10 年估值均值作为参考，光模
块、半导体制造、电⽹与配电等板块仍需上修盈利 40~70% ，才能匹配当前定价，⽽云⼚商盈利
则保有近 20% 的盈利下修空间。

图表 15 ：整体美股估值当前并不在极端⾼位，但 AI 产业链内部存在明显分化
资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部
图表 16 ：光模块、半导体制造、电⽹与配电等计⼊⾼增预期较多的板块，需要上修盈利
40~70% ，云⼚商则为后续盈利增⻓保留了近 20% 的空间
资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部

### Page 1

⻢斯克最新表态： " 我爱中国！ " 他盛赞中国汽⻋⼯业竞争⼒，并称特斯拉上海⼯⼚的成功有
赖于中国团队。与此同时，⻢斯克放出豪⾔， SpaceX 年化营收有望在 2027 年前突破 5000
亿美元，甚⾄冲击万亿规模，远超华尔街预期。
摘要：
当地时间 11 ⽉ 6 ⽇，特斯拉公司⾸席执⾏官⻢斯克接受投资者罗恩 · 巴伦采访时表⽰，他热爱中
国，并⾼度评价中国汽⻋⼯业的竞争⼒。
“ 中国令⼈惊叹。顺便说⼀句，我爱中国。去那⾥旅⾏令⼈深受⿎舞， ” ⻢斯克在采访中说。
⻢斯克指出，在上海⽣产的特斯拉 Model Y 是中国最畅销的电动汽⻋，实现这⼀成绩 “ 并不容
易，因为中国汽⻋公司⾮常厉害。 ”
这不是⻢斯克第⼀次点赞中国。此前他也曾公开表⽰ “ 中国的魅⼒⽆以⾔表！ ”“ 中国制造业底蕴
深厚，中国的 AI ⼤模型⾮常出⾊！ ”
⻢斯克曾透露，中国具备极强的制造业底蕴。特斯拉上海超级⼯⼚的成功，靠的是中国团队。
⻢斯克也多次在社交平台向⽹友推荐中国： “ 中国太棒了。我强烈⿎励⼤家去旅游。 ”

此外，⻢斯克周五（ 6 ⽇）表⽰，到 2027 年 12 ⽉， SpaceX 的年化营收有望突破 5000 亿美元。这
⼀预测远⾼于华尔街分析师对这家⽕箭、卫星和⼈⼯智能（ AI ）公司的预期。
他还表⽰， SpaceX 年化营收达到 1 万亿美元的规模也并⾮不可能。
周五， SpaceX 股价上涨 8.62% ，报 134.27 美元，收复了周⼆中期选举后连续两⽇下跌的部分失
地。不过，该股仍低于今年 6 ⽉上市时 135 美元的发⾏价。

Document 651: 凱基投顧_8210 勤誠_余昀澄_20261106

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_8210 勤誠_余昀澄_20261106.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 06, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  電子零組件 ‧ 台灣

勤誠 
(8210.TW /8210 TT)

產品轉換期壓抑短期營收與獲利率  
重要訊息 
3Q26 EPS 8.06 元，低於凱基預估及市場共識，主因 毛利率下滑。因主要專
案設計變更影響出貨，我們預期 4Q26 營收與利潤率將低於我們 原估。 
評論及分析  
3Q26 EPS 低於預期。 3Q26 EPS 年增 1%、季減 27%至 8.06 元，低於凱基
預估 14%、市場共識 29%，主因毛利率低於預期 。毛利率季減 6.8ppts 至
25.8%，低於凱基預估 3.2ppts，主因 NPI 專案較少使 NRE 收入下滑、HGX
營收比重降低 使產品組合不利 ，以及產能利用率下降。儘管費用率 7.4%低
於凱基預估，但營利率 仍季減 6.1ppts 至 18.6%，低於凱基預估 2.2ppts。 
4Q26 營收低於預期。 10 月營收季減 7%至 23.08 億元，低於凱基預估及市
場共識，主因 ASIC 與 HGX 營收疲弱。根據供應鏈訪查 ，ASIC 客戶因製程
優化需求而調整機構件設計，目前勤誠正與客戶合作進行機殼設計變更，我
們預期 11 月營收仍可能維持疲弱， 須待 12 月營收才較明顯回溫。我們 因此
將 4Q26 ASIC 營收預估由季增下修至季持平。此外，我們預估 4Q26 HGX
營收將季減，亦低於原先預期，僅 GB/VR 機殼與機櫃營收可望季增。我們下
修 4Q26 營收至季減 2%。考量產能利用率持續偏低、 NRE 有限、產品組合
不利，下修 4Q26 毛利率預估至 25.7% (季減)，同步下修 4Q26 EPS 至 7.52
元，以及 2026 年 EPS 至 37.4 元，年增 29%。 
2027 年營收仍成長，但毛利率低於預期。 勤誠 2027 年主要營收成長動能包
括：(1) ASIC 伺服器機殼放量；(2) 市占率提升使 Nvidia (美) GB300/VR200 
伺服器機殼營收成長；(3) AMD ( 美) Helios 機櫃、CDU 專案及客製化機櫃出
貨；與 (4) 切入 AI 基礎設施機構件業務。然而，我們下修 HGX 與降噪櫃營
收，2027 年整體營收下修至年增 25%。我們預估 1H27 毛利率仍維持低
檔，須待 2H27 新專案放量才會使毛利率較明顯回溫。因產品組合不利 (ASIC
與機櫃比重提高) 、新廠上線使折舊費用增加，我們 下修 2027 年毛利率至
26.6%，下修 2027 年 EPS 至 41元，年增 10%。 
投資建議  
我們預期 1H27 獲利維持年減，須至 3Q27 才恢復年增，維持「持有」評等。
反映獲利預估下修，下修目標價至 615元，基於 15x (原 18x) 2027F EPS。 
投資風險  
伺服器需求低迷、新專案遞延、總體經濟不確定性。

圖 5: 公司概況  圖 6: 1-3Q26 美系客戶營收比重 提升至 67% 
勤誠興業創立於 1983 年，為機櫃式、塔式伺服器及個人電腦
機構方案設計製造商。 2025 年客戶別營收比重則為美國客戶
60%、中國客戶 30%、歐洲客戶 7%、其他地區 3%。勤誠近
年成功擴展業務至資料中心與工業解決方案，並持續提高市佔
率以及提升營收。

Document 677: 刚押注AI就遭遇市场暴击！伯克希尔今晚发布财报，阿贝尔转型成色迎大考

Source PDF: all report pdf/刚押注AI就遭遇市场暴击！伯克希尔今晚发布财报，阿贝尔转型成色迎大考.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Greg Abel 执掌伯克希尔不⾜⼀年，便以逾 109 亿美元押注 AI 赛道，与巴菲特的科技股回避
传统彻底决裂。然⽽ Alphabet 股价已跌破建仓成本， Bending Spoons 持续回落， AI 豪赌
正遭现实检验。与此同时，三季度每股收益预计同⽐下滑 6% ，回购规模料从 45 亿美元骤降
⾄ 16 亿美元， 3580 亿美元现⾦如何部署，考验这位新掌⻔⼈的资本配置功⼒。
摘要：
伯克希尔哈撒⻙公司将于今晚发布第三季度财报，这是 CEO Greg Abel 执掌这家万亿美元级别企
业以来最受瞩⽬的⼀次业绩披露。在他⼤举押注⼈⼯智能之后，相关投资随即遭遇市场逆⻛，
外界对这位新掌⻔⼈的战略判断与资本配置能⼒的审视，正在达到前所未有的⾼度。
Abel 上任以来最引⼈注⽬的动作，是向⾕歌⺟公司 Alphabet 投⼊逾 100 亿美元、向意⼤利科技集
团 Bending Spoons 投⼊ 9.3 亿美元，由此完成伯克希尔历史上最⼤规模的 AI 主题押注。然⽽，
Alphabet 股价⾃ 6 ⽉以来已累计下跌逾 24% ，⽬前已跌破伯克希尔的建仓成本； Bending

Spoons 股价在伯克希尔加仓后短暂冲⾼，随后在过去三周持续回落。这⼀系列在 AI 领域投资的
时机正受到市场质疑。
此外，市场对本次财报的关注焦点还集中在其他三个维度：每股收益的下滑幅度、回购⼒度能
否延续，以及现⾦储备的消耗节奏。据 FactSet 数据，第三季度每股 A 类股运营收益预计同⽐下
降约 6% ⾄ 8821 美元；据 Visible Alpha 估计，季末现⾦储备料从⼆季度末的 3655 亿美元降⾄
3580 亿美元；回购⽅⾯，市场预期约为 16.4 亿美元，远低于⼆季度的 45 亿美元。
AI 押注遭遇现实检验
Abel 接掌伯克希尔不⾜⼀年，便以两笔⼤⼿笔投资宣告与前任 Warren Buffett 的科技股回避传统
决裂。据彭博报道，他向 Alphabet 投⼊逾 100 亿美元以⽀持其 AI 相关业务，并向 Bending
Spoons 投⼊ 9.3 亿美元 —— 后者是⼀家利⽤ AI 技术推动企业转型的意⼤利集团。
然⽽市场的反应令⼈警醒。 Alphabet A 类和 C 类股票⾃ 6 ⽉以来已双双下跌逾 24% ，当前股价已

低于伯克希尔的买⼊价格，伯克希尔在这笔投资上很可能已处于浮亏状态。 Bending Spoons ⽅
⾯，伯克希尔在其 7 ⽉ IPO 时买⼊ 6 亿美元，随后⼜通过私募⽅式追加 3.3 亿美元，但股价在短暂
上涨后已连续三周下跌。
CFRA Research 分析师 Cathy Seifert 表⽰，" 我确实认为这在⼀定程度上是⼀个有意识地提升 AI
敞⼝的决定，但⼈们不得不追问：是否已经有些晚了？ "
不过，也有分析师持更为⻓远的视⻆。 Keefe Bruyette & Woods 分析师 Meyer Shields 认为，
Abel 很可能将这些投资视为对 AI 技术⻓期潜⼒的战略押注， " 他并不是在向市场传递信号，他可
以以伯克希尔⼀贯的保守主义来审视这些投资。 "Shields 还指出， Buffett2016 年投资苹果公司时
同样饱受质疑，最终却成为伯克希尔最丰厚的回报之⼀。
投资者还需等到 11 ⽉中旬才能知晓， Abel 是否借助市场对 AI 的担忧情绪及估值回落，进⼀步加

仓相关标的。
每股收益承压，保险与铁路成关键变量
第三季度每股 A 类股运营收益（剔除股权投资损益）预计同⽐下降约 6% ⾄ 8821 美元，主要拖累
来⾃财险市场竞争加剧导致的承保利润收窄。不过， UBS 分析师 Brian Meredith 持相对乐观⽴
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
场，预计受⼯业业务⽀撑，运营利润将录得约 3% 的增⻓。
保险板块中， Geico 的表现备受关注。上⼀季度， Geico 在同业普遍盈利的背景下逆势录得利润
下滑，原因在于赔付成本上升及⼴告⽀出增加。 Shields 表⽰， " 整体个⼈⻋险市场竞争趋于理
性，我认为我们将看到 Geico 采取更为积极的增⻓策略。 " 据 Visible Alpha 估计，第三季度保费收
⼊预计同⽐微增 0.6% ⾄ 226 亿美元，其中 Geico 保费收⼊预计为 114 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 1.5% ；保
险承保收⼊共识预期为 14.7 亿美元，同⽐⼤幅下降 54% 。
铁路业务⽅⾯，分析师认为强劲的货运量数据及燃油附加费有望⽀撑 BNSF 的业绩表现。Abel 已
将提升 BNSF 利润率列为公司优先战略⽬标，该业务⽬前的利润率仍低于多数同业竞争对⼿。
此外，据巴伦周刊报道，伯克希尔的⽇元借款在⽇元汇率⾛强的背景下，可能带来数亿美元的
汇兑损失，并将按照 GAAP 准则计⼊运营利润，对报告数字形成⼀定拖累。

回购⼒度或降温，现⾦部署仍是核⼼议题
回购动向历来是伯克希尔财报季的核⼼看点之⼀，因为它直接反映管理层对股票内在价值的判
断。⼆季度，伯克希尔回购了 45 亿美元股票，创下近四年来的单季最⾼纪录；据巴伦周刊测
算，仅 7 ⽉前四周的回购规模便约达 34 亿美元，彼时 A 类股均价低于 75 万美元，约为账⾯价值的
1.4 倍。
然⽽，随着股价持续攀升，回购空间已明显收窄。⽬前 A 类股已涨⾄ 87.5 万美元的历史⾼位，过
去⼀个多⽉累计上涨 16% ，市净率约为 1.6 倍，接近近年来的估值上限。据 Visible Alpha 估计，
第三季度回购规模约为 16.4 亿美元。
Seifert 表⽰， " 如果近期股价的上涨已经抑制了他们回购的热情，我认为投资者可能会感到有些
失望。 "
Seeking Alpha 分析师 Louis Gerard 则设定了更为明确的观察标准：若回购低于 20 亿美元且现⾦
储备同步上升，或 B 类股在账⾯价值未有相应提升的情况下突破 570 美元，他将把评级从买⼊下

调⾄持有。
在现⾦储备⽅⾯，⼆季度末伯克希尔持有约 3655 亿美元的现⾦、现⾦等价物及短期国债。三季
度的主要消耗包括： 7 ⽉以约 68 亿美元完成对住宅建筑商 Taylor Morrison 的收购，以及 9 ⽉末建
⽴约 21.6 亿美元的 Lennar 股权仓位。据 Visible Alpha 估计，季末现⾦储备料降⾄ 3580 亿美元。
如何进⼀步部署这笔庞⼤的现⾦，仍是众多投资者对 Abel 最迫切的期待。

### Page 3

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

住宅着工・許可件数 
住宅着工 住宅許可 
米国 26 年 9 月住宅着工はパンデミック以来の低水準 
～予想外の 3 カ月連続減少、トランプ政権の関税・高金利が悪影響～ 
 経済調査部 主任エコノミスト  桂畑 誠治 
（℡：050-5474-7493）

要旨  
・2026 年 9 月の米住宅着工件数は季節調整済・年率換算で 122.1 万戸（前月比▲5.2%）と 3
カ月連続で減少した。市場予想中央値（130.5 万戸への増加）に反し、パンデミック初期の 
2020 年 5 月（103.9 万戸）以来、約 6 年 4 カ月ぶりの低水準に落ち込んだ。 
・30 年固定住宅ローン金利が高止まり（6.8%前後）する中でアフォーダビリティ（住宅購買 
力）が悪化している。これに加え、トランプ政権下での関税強化（カナダ産木材等の資材高）や 
不法移民取り締まり強化に伴う建設現場の人手不足・人件費高騰が構造的コスト高となり、供給 
側の着工意欲を強力に抑制している。 
・2026 年 7-9 月期の実質民間住宅投資は前期比年率▲2.0％程度となり、GDP成長率を約 
0.1％ポイント押し下げる公算が大きい。2026 年通年の着工件数は前年比▲1.2%程度の減少が 
予想され、高金利環境の長期化やFRBによる追加利上げリスクにより、住宅市場の本格回復は大
きく後ずれする見通しである。

1．住宅 着工 は市場予想 に反して 3 カ月連続で減少 、2020 年 5 月以来の低水準  
2026 年 9 月の住宅着工件数（季節調整済、年率換算）は 122.1 万戸となり、前月比▲5.2％（前 
月改定値：128.8 万戸、同▲5.9％）と、市場予想中央値（ブルームバーグ集計）の 130.5 万戸 
（同＋1.9％）に反して 3 カ月連続の減少となった。これは新型コロナパンデミック初期の 2020

（図表）住宅着工件数の推移 （万戸） 
(出所）米商務省 
（万戸） 
年 5 月（103.9 万戸）以来、約 6 年 4 カ月ぶりの低い水準である。なお、7･8 月分は計 7.3 万戸上
方修正されたものの、下落基調を覆すには至っていない。 
内訳をみると、全体の 7 割近くを占め着工の基調を示す「一戸建て住宅着工件数」が 85.9 万
戸、前月比▲4.6％（前月改定値：90.0 万戸、同＋2.9％）と再び減少した。30 年固定住宅ローン 
金利が 6.8％前後で高止まりしたことによるアフォーダビリティ（住宅購買力）の低下と住宅価格 
の高騰が購買層の資金力を圧迫していることに加え、資材コスト高や現場の労働力不足が根強い 
下振れ要因となっている。 
また、月次での変動が激しい「集合住宅着工件数」は 36.2 万戸、前月比▲6.7％（前月改定 
値：38.8 万戸、同▲21.5％）と 3 カ月連続で減少した。6 月に一時的な反動で急増した後、調整 
が続いている。低金利ローンを維持する既存住宅オーナーの売り控え（ロックイン効果）に伴う 
新築需要の底堅さは存在するものの、過去数年間に供給された物件の完成・入居が進む中で賃貸 
市場の需給が緩んでおり、開発事業者の新規着工意欲は極めて低調である。 
地域別では、全米の約半数を占める最大市場である南部が増加（前月比＋6.1％）した一方、中 
西部（同▲19.0％）や西部（同▲23.2％）が大幅に減少した。北東部は前月比 0.0％にとどまり、
南部以外の地域で着工が足踏みした。

2．許可件数は 減少し 、建設業者の慎重姿勢で 底位推移   
着工の先行指標となる住宅建設許可件数（季節調整済、年率換算）は、9 月に 140.0 万戸、前月 
比▲0.2％（前月改定値：140.3 万戸、同▲2.1％）と減少したものの、市場予想中央値(ブルーム 
バーグ集計)の 138.5 万戸（同▲1.3％）を上回った。なお、7･8 月分は計 0.9 万戸上方修正されて
いる。 
内訳をみると、一戸建てが 84.5 万戸、前月比▲4.0％（前月改定値：88.0 万戸、同▲1.6％）と
2 カ月連続で減少し、変動の激しい集合住宅は 55.5 万戸、前月比＋6.1％（前月改定値：52.3 万

### Page 3

戸、同▲3.0％）と増加に転じた。 
先行指標である許可件数の低迷は、先行きに対する建設業界の慎重姿勢の強まりを反映してい
る。全米住宅建設業協会（NAHB）住宅市場指数などのマインド指標も 10 月に 29 まで低下し、パ
ンデミック直後の水準を下回り、2012 年以来の最低水準となり、判断の分かれ目である 50 を大き
く下回る状態が続いている。 
特に注目すべきは、 「許可取得済み未着工件数」が高水準に留まっている点である。新築住宅価 
格の高止まりで未販売在庫が増加する中、関税や労働コストの上昇を見越した建設業者が実際の 
着工を先送りする「パイプラインの目詰まり」が生じており、許可件数が着工へとスムーズに結 
びつきにくい構造が浮き彫りとなっている。地域別では、北東部（前月比＋13.3％） 、南部（同＋
1.5％） 、中西部（同＋0.5％）で増加した一方、西部が同▲10.2％と減少した。

3．住宅市場は 高金利と政策リスクによる不確実性の高まり を背景に 停滞感 が漂う  
住宅市場全体には、住宅ローン金利の高止まりと価格上昇に伴う実質的な住宅買い控え（販売 
停滞感）が色濃く漂っている。これに加え、トランプ政権による関税引き上げ（カナダ産木材等 
の建材価格押し上げ）や不法移民取り締まり強化（建設現場での作業員不足と人件費高騰）に対 
する強い懸念が、供給側の構造的コスト圧迫リスクとなっている。 
建設業者はローン金利を一時的に引き下げる「モーゲージ・ブイダウン（金利肩代わり特約） 」
などのインセンティブ提供で需要喚起を図っているものの、これら販促費用の上昇に伴う粗利益 
率の低下から、新規着工に対して慎重な姿勢を崩していない。買い手の需要減退と売り手のコス 
ト高・利益圧迫が同時に進行しており、住宅投資の停滞はマクロ経済全体へ負の波及効果を及ぼ 
している。今回の着工・進捗の鈍化により、2026 年 7-9 月期の民間住宅投資は前期比年率▲2％程 
度となり、GDP成長率に対して約▲0.1％ポイントの押し下げ寄与となる公算が大きい。

なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。

4．先行き は需要 鈍化 と供給制約 によ り停滞 が継続
2026 年後半から 2027 年にかけての住宅市場は、実質所得の改善や建設業者による販促策（ブイ
ダウン特約の提供など）が一定の下支えとなるものの、FRBによる追加利上げの織り込みや財政赤
字拡大懸念に伴う長期金利の押し上げを受けた住宅ローン金利の高止まり、および住宅価格の高
価格化が需要の重しとなり、住宅販売は弱含みで推移する見通しである。
また、関税政策や移民規制に伴う「資材高・人手不足」という二重の供給制約により、建設業

者の投資意欲は抑えられ続ける公算が大きい。このため、2026 年の住宅着工件数は前年比▲1.2％
程度の減少が見込まれ、2025 年（前年比▲1.0％）に続く減少傾向が継続すると考えられる。現在
の利上げサイクルの終了や追加利上げ懸念の後退、インフレの低下が見込まれる 2027 年半ばごろ
までは本格的な回復は見通しにくいものの、同時期以降に緩やかな底打ち・回復へ向かうと想定
される。

美国中期选举落地；资金面明显转宽，债市延续强势
【内容摘要】11月 4日，资金面明显转宽；债市延续强势；转债市场主要指数集体跟涨，转
债个券多数上涨；各期限美债收益率普遍下行，主要欧洲经济体10年期国债收益率普遍下行。
一、债市要闻
（一）国内要闻
【央行加力实施股票回购增持再贷款政策】11月 4日，央行公告，自11月 4日起，下调股票
回购增持再贷款利率0.25个百分点至 1%；支持对象为 21家全国性银行和 5家系统重要性城
商行，支持比例 100%，期限 1年，可根据银行需要展期；银行可在再贷款利率基础上适当加
点，合理确定股票回购增持贷款期限和利率，加快贷款投放，增强上市公司、主要股东市值管
理能力。11月 2日股票回购增持再贷款座谈会之后，银行已向央行报送股票回购增持再贷款
额度申请超过2000亿元。
【国家发改委：推动更多水利基础设施投资信托基金发行上市】11月 4日，国家发改委有关
负责人在奋进“十五五”系列新闻发布会上表示，“十五五”时期，国家发改委将采取更加有
力有效的政策措施，稳交通投资、稳经济大盘。努力拓宽水利投融资渠道，推动更多水利基础

设施投资信托基金（REITs）发行上市。国家发改委基础设施发展司司长夏鑫表示，“十五五”
时期，国家发改委将坚持适度超前、不过度超前，以推进基础设施一体衔接、协同融合为导向，
加强统筹规划，加快构建先进适用、系统完备、集约高效、安全绿色的现代化基础设施体系。
【五部门印发《实施全面节约战略行动方案》】国家发改委等五部门近日联合印发《实施全面
节约战略行动方案》，提出了到2030年全面节约工作的主要目标，聚焦“能水粮地矿材”各
类资源要素节约面临的利用方式粗放、利用效率不高等突出问题，打出一套“组合拳”。到2030
年，我国单位国内生产总值能耗、用水量、建设用地使用面积比2025年分别降低 10%左右、
10%、15%，粮食全产业链各环节损失率持续降低，主要再生资源年循环利用量达到5.1亿吨，
进一步形成崇尚节约的社会风尚。鼓励金融机构通过绿色信贷、绿色债券等金融工具加强对资
源节约相关项目的支持。引导和规范社会资本参与相关项目投资、建设、运营。
【交易商协会：鼓励企业发行科技创新债券、绿色债务融资工具、并购票据等用于先进制造

### Page 2

业】11月 4日，交易商协会发布《关于银行间债券市场支持先进制造业发展的通知》。其中
提到，鼓励企业发行科技创新债券、绿色债务融资工具、并购票据等用于先进制造业；支持以
应收账款、知识产权(含专利权)、融资租赁债权等资产发行先进制造业资产证券化产品；支持
依托先进制造业产业链开展供应链应收账款、供应链票据资产证券化；鼓励针对先进制造业企
业特点，灵活设计发行条款；鼓励特定区域及产业链上的企业集合发行，服务先进制造业集群、
战略性新兴产业集群和创新型产业集群发展。
（二）国际要闻
【美民主党重掌国会，特朗普执政承重压】多家美国媒体预测，美国民主党在当地时间11月
3日举行的中期选举中力压共和党夺回参议院和众议院控制权。美国舆论普遍认为，特朗普政
府未来两年的施政空间将明显收窄，可能在很多方面不得不作出妥协甚至与民主党合作。美国
全国广播公司最新预测显示，民主党或将在众议院赢得超过250个席位，超过 218席的多数
门槛，实现“大翻盘”。媒体还预测，民主党连续在多个关键州击败共和党获得国会参议院席

位，包括南卡罗来纳州、得克萨斯州和堪萨斯州等共和党传统票仓。美国总统特朗普对美国媒
体表示，伊朗战事对共和党在中期选举中的表现“有一定影响”，但他并不后悔发动伊朗战事。
【美国 10月 ISM服务业 PMI创三个月最低，售价涨幅创四年最快，加息预期上升】11月 4
日，美国供应管理协会（ISM）公布的数据显示，美国10月服务业指数环比回落0.4个百分点
至 54.5，低于市场预期的54.8。同时，10月标普全球美国服务业PMI从58.8降至 56.1，双
双令市场失望。尽管两项指标均高于荣枯线，但增速明显收窄。通胀信号尤为值得警惕。据标
普全球美国服务业 PMI报告，10月服务业平均收费价格涨幅升至近四年来最快水平之一，叠
加制造业 PMI所反映的商品价格上涨，市场普遍预计未来数月整体通胀将进一步偏离美联储
目标，12月加息概率随之走高。
【美国财政部修改美债发行指引，或为2027年削减长期国债铺路】11月4日，美国财政部在
季度再融资声明中宣布，未来数个季度将维持名义附息债券及浮息票据拍卖规模不变，但删去

了自 2024年初以来一直沿用的“至少”一词。这一细微措辞变化意味着财政部已预留空间，
最早可于2027年对长期债券发行规模进行下调。此举是贝森特在过去数月间持续推进的一系
列操作之一。就在8月再融资声明发布仅两周后，美国财政部意外大幅扩大国债回购规模，但
该举措未能遏制长端收益率上行，30年期美债收益率上月一度升至2002年以来最高水平。

Document 897: 都区部版・消費者物価のコア指標（2026_10） ～底堅く推移、日銀は2％到達を表明へ～

Source PDF: all report pdf/都区部版・消費者物価のコア指標（2026_10） ～底堅く推移、日銀は2％到達を表明へ～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年 10 月 30 日

1/3 
都区部版・消費者物価のコア指標（2026/10） 
～底堅く推移、日銀は 2％到達を表明へ～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  星野 卓也 
（℡：050-5474-7497）

図表１．消費者物価のコア指標：筆者試算の都区部版、日銀試算の全国版（前年比・％）

（注１） 日銀公表値に倣う形で、以下で各値を求めている。刈込平均値：価格品目別上昇率分布の上下10％（ウェイトベース）を除
去して前年比を算出、加重中央値：価格品目別上昇率分布における50％近傍の値の前年比（ウェイトベース） 、最頻値：各
品目別の価格上昇率分布から最尤法でパラメトリックに分布を推定（正規逆ガウス分布） 。得られた分布の最頻値。 
（注２） 低変動品目CPI の作成方法は尾崎・神保・八木・吉井（2024）に倣っている。品目別CPI の標準偏差の大小で３つにグルー
ピングを行い、標準偏差の小さいグループの上昇率を示す。 「低変動CPI_東京」については、全国版の品目グルーピングで
都区部CPI を集計した値。 
（注３） 都区部版における特殊要因については、日銀と同様の消費税率の変更、教育無償化政策、携帯電話通信料引き下げ、旅行支
援策、ガソリン、電気ガス代の負担緩和策のほか、都独自の政策である水道料金無償化、保育料無償化を織り込んだ。 
（注４） 日銀は一部品目・影響の除去を行っているが本試算では行っていない等、一部手法に異なる点がある点に留意。直近値は東
京都区部の中旬速報値、過去値は確定値より作成。2025 年12 月まで2020 年基準、2026 年1 月～2025 年基準CPI。 
（出所）総務省、日本銀行より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所が作成。

### Page 2

2/3
底堅く推移。日銀は基調的物価2％到達を表明へ
本日公表された 10 月の東京都区部 CPI を用いて、日銀が全国 CPI を用いて試算している「消費
者物価のコア指標」等に準拠する形で、①エネルギー補助金等の政策要因を除いた「特殊要因除く
系列」 、 ②東京都区部版の刈込平均、 加重中央、 最頻値、③日銀が賃金から物価への波及度合いを分
析する際に利用した「低変動品目CPI」について計算した。
計算値をみると、生鮮・特殊要因除くは9 月：＋3.0％→10 月：＋3.2％、生鮮エネ・特殊要因除
くは 9 月：＋3.0％→10 月：＋2.8％、食料エネ特殊要因除くは 9 月：＋2.7％→10 月：＋2.6％、
刈込平均値（全国ウェイト換算）は9 月：＋2.3％→10 月：＋2.4％、加重中央値（全国ウェイト換
算） は9 月：＋2.0％→10 月：＋1.9％、 最頻値は9 月：＋1.6％→10 月：＋1.8％となった。 また、
全国版の低変動品目 CPI は 8 月：＋1.3％→9 月：＋1.4％、都区部では 9 月：＋2.0％→10 月：＋

2.0％となった（以上、いずれも前年比）。
方向感は指標によって分かれたが、 全般的に物価の底堅さを確認する内容といって良い。 日銀は
今日の会合で基調的な物価の 2％到達を判断すると報じられている。本稿の特殊要因除く系、分布
系指標の強さのほか、 予想インフレ率 （弊著「合成予想インフレ率の動向 （2026Q3）」なども参照）
が概ね 2％に達したことを根拠にした判断とみられる。 今後の金融政策は 「2％を目指す」 局面から
「2％に落ち着ける」局面にシフトすることになる。日銀は基調的インフレ率指標群を「2％を上抜
けないか」を確認する指標として重視することになりそうである。
実際の物価は、今後原油価格上昇分の転嫁が進むことで伸び率を高める可能性が高い。 食料品や
日用品価格を含むナウキャスト社の 「日経CPINOW」 は直近値で＋3.6％（10 月 27 日時点、7 日移動
平均）と都区部CPI 調査時点の10 月上中旬の平均（＋2.9％）から伸びを高めている。今後、特に
重要なのはこの実際物価の上昇が中長期の予想インフレ率に跳ねてくるかどうかである。直近7-9

月期の合成予想インフレ率は家計・企業で一服感がみられたが、 実際物価の上昇を受けて予想イン
フレ率への波及がみられるかを日銀は特に注視していくことになるだろう。

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。

Document 906: 风险正在转移！高盛：芯片商CDS利差首次超过超大云厂商，AI违约掉期创纪录

Source PDF: all report pdf/风险正在转移！高盛：芯片商CDS利差首次超过超大云厂商，AI违约掉期创纪录.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

⾼盛表⽰， AI 信贷⻛险正从云巨头下移⾄芯⽚供应商，芯⽚ CDS 利差近三个⽉⾸次超越超
⼤规模云⼚商，反映出市场对 GPU 折旧与客⼾融资敞⼝加剧的隐忧。受供给潮冲击，云⼚
商发债溢价显著攀升且久期缩短。随着 2027 年数千亿美元项⽬融资涌⼊，信贷市场压⼒仍
将持续累积。
摘要：
AI 产业链的债务⻛险，正在从⼤型云计算公司向芯⽚供应商转移。
据⾼盛销售与交易部⻔上周⽇发布的四份信贷报告，由博通、 AMD 、英伟达和英特尔组成的芯
⽚公司信⽤违约互换篮⼦（ CDS ，可理解为市场对企业违约⻛险的定价）利差，近三个⽉来⾸
次超过了甲⻣⽂、 Meta 、亚⻢逊、 Alphabet 、微软等超⼤规模云⼚商的 CDS 篮⼦。
这意味着，投资者开始更担⼼芯⽚公司要承担的⻛险：⼀边是⾼价图形处理器（ GPU ）更新换
代加快、折旧压⼒上升，另⼀边则是芯⽚⼚商通过融资、担保等⽅式⽀持下游客⼾扩张。市场
正在追问：如果算⼒投资回报不及预期，最终由谁承担设备贬值和融资损失？
与此同时，AI 相关违约⻛险合约的单周成交额已升⾄ 80 亿⾄ 100 亿美元，约为⼀年前的七倍。⾼

盛信贷交易员 Alisha Pasi 直⾔： “ ⻛险正在迁移。 ”
芯⽚商⻛险为何突然上升？
最直接的信号来⾃违约⻛险定价的变化。
今年 8 ⽉初，芯⽚公司 CDS 利差约为 80 个基点，⽐⼤型云⼚商低约 20 个基点；三个⽉后，这⼀利
差已翻倍⾄约 160 个基点，并⾸次超过云⼚商。利差上升，通常意味着市场认为企业未来的偿债
⻛险正在增加。
从个股看，博通的 CDS 利差较两个⽉前低点扩⼤约 70 个基点， AMD 扩⼤ 55 个基点，英伟达扩⼤
50 个基点。相⽐之下， Meta 和 Alphabet 仅扩⼤约 15 个基点，微软⼏乎没有明显变化。
上周，在美国投资级企业违约⻛险指数中，利差扩⼤最多的三家公司也全部来⾃芯⽚⾏业：英
特尔扩⼤ 40 个基点⾄ 141 个基点， AMD 扩⼤ 26.5 个基点⾄ 147 个基点，博通扩⼤ 17.6 个基点⾄
186 个基点。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

这反映出市场对 AI ⻛险链条的重新定价：⼤型云⼚商虽然资本开⽀巨⼤，但拥有更稳定的现⾦
流、更强的资产负债表和更⾼的融资能⼒；⽽芯⽚供应商⾯临的⻛险更复杂 —— 它们既要承受
⾼端芯⽚快速迭代带来的折旧压⼒，也可能通过客⼾融资、订单承诺等⽅式间接承担下游需求
⻛险。
核⼼问题变了：谁来承接不断增加的芯⽚？
⾼盛认为，2027 年可能成为 AI 融资⻛险的关键压⼒测试。届时，市场预计将迎来超过 1500 亿美
元、以特殊⽬的载体为主体的算⼒融资。
所谓特殊⽬的载体融资，通常是将 GPU 、数据中⼼设备或电⼒合同等资产打包，以未来租赁现
⾦流为基础进⾏融资。问题在于，这类融资的抵押品并⾮传统⼚房或⼟地，⽽是技术迭代速度
极快的 GPU 。
⼀旦新⼀代芯⽚推出、算⼒价格下降，旧设备的残值可能迅速缩⽔；如果下游客⼾的订单、租
赁收⼊或融资能⼒⼜出现问题，⻛险就可能从数据中⼼运营商进⼀步传导⾄芯⽚供应商。
因此，市场关注点正在从 “ 云计算巨头还要借多少钱 ” ，转向 “ 这些巨头和数据中⼼运营商购买的

芯⽚，最终由谁承担折旧和回款⻛险 ” 。
云⼚商仍在⼤量发债，但融资成本明显上升
⻛险并不意味着⼤型云⼚商已经安全⽆虞。相反，它们仍是 AI 融资潮中最主要的发债主体，只是
市场暂时认为其抗⻛险能⼒强于芯⽚供应商。
上周，美国投资级公司债发⾏规模约 760 亿美元，接近市场预期的两倍，成为 2026 年第四⼤发债
周，也是 11 ⽉历史上规模最⼤的⼀周。年初⾄今，美国投资级债券发⾏总额已突破 2.079 万亿美
元，创下纪录。
其中，超⼤规模云⼚商是主要推动⼒量。仅从 10 ⽉ 29 ⽇以来，市场就出现五笔来⾃云计算巨头
的独⽴债券交易，合计规模约 670 亿美元。全球超⼤规模云⼚商年内发债总额已达到 2960 亿美
元，明显超过⾼盛此前预测的 2500 亿美元。
不过，投资者消化这轮供给潮的代价正在提⾼。年初⾄今，新债发⾏利差平均为 3.4 个基点，⽽
近期已升⾄ 7.8 个基点； 11 ⽉ AI 相关债券的平均新债发⾏利差更超过 18 个基点，显著⾼于⾮ AI 企
业债。
部分投资级 AI 债券的收益率，甚⾄已经⾼于⾼收益债指数中 83% 的债券。这意味着，虽然这些发

⾏⼈信⽤评级仍较⾼，但投资者已开始要求更⾼回报，以补偿 AI 资本开⽀和债务扩张带来的不确
定性。
云⼚商不愿锁定⻓期⾼成本债务
另⼀个值得注意的变化是，云⼚商正在缩短发债期限。
例如， Meta 近期 150 亿美元发债中， 30 年期债券仅占 27.5 亿美元，并⾸次没有发⾏ 40 年期债
券；甲⻣⽂ 100 亿美元发债的最⻓期限仅为 20 年，没有 30 年或 40 年期品种；苹果 75 亿美元发债
同样未发⾏ 30 年期债券。
这说明，⽆论是发⾏⼈还是投资者，对于超⻓期 AI 债务都更为谨慎。发⾏⼈不愿在利率较⾼时锁
定⻓期融资成本，投资者也不愿过度暴露于 AI 投资回报尚未完全验证的远期⻛险。

### Page 3

⾼收益债市场已先感受到压⼒
⻛险向芯⽚和数据中⼼环节扩散，也在⾼收益债市场中体现得更加明显。
⾼盛数据显⽰，上周其参与交易的 29 只 AI 相关⾼收益债券平均下跌 1.67 点，⽽其他⾼收益债券
平均上涨 0.36 点。
在信⽤评级较⾼的 BB 级债券中， AI 和数据中⼼相关债券平均下跌 1.52 点，约为 BB 级能源债跌幅
的 4.5 倍。尽管数据中⼼债券在 BB 级指数中的占⽐只有 6.7% ，不到能源板块 14.3% 占⽐的⼀半，
但其对指数表现的拖累却是能源板块的两倍。
此外，数据中⼼扩张还⾯临政策阻⼒。⾼盛统计显⽰，上周美国共有 41 场地⽅选举涉及数据中
⼼禁建、监管收紧或具体项⽬审批，其中 40 场结果对数据中⼼建设不利。⾼盛经济学家估计，
短期禁建措施可能影响美国约 45% 的计划建设产能。
这意味着，数据中⼼项⽬⾯临的不只是融资成本上升，还包括建设审批、供电保障、社区阻⼒
和项⽬延期等现实⻛险。
股票市场⼤跌，信贷市场却异常平静
值得警惕的是，股票与债券市场近期出现了明显背离。

上周四，标普 500 指数单⽇下跌 4.4% ，纳斯达克市场市值蒸发约 2.5 万亿美元；但投资级企业违
约⻛险指数当天仅扩⼤ 3.5 个基点，远低于历史上股市出现类似跌幅时的正常反应。
从全周看，标普 500 指数下跌 2.7% ，投资级信⽤利差仅扩⼤ 1 个基点，⾼收益信⽤利差甚⾄收窄
4 个基点，杠杆贷款市场则录得今年 4 ⽉以来最佳单周表现。
⾼盛交易员认为，这种 “ 股跌债稳 ” 未必代表⻛险已经消失，反⽽可能意味着信贷市场尚未充分反
映 AI 投资周期中积累的压⼒。表⾯上看，信⽤指数仍较平稳；但在个券层⾯， AI 、芯⽚、数据中
⼼等⾏业的分化已经明显加剧。
2027 年或迎来融资压⼒集中释放
⾼盛预计，随着超⼤规模云⼚商⾃由现⾦流转负，债务融资已承担其 2026 年资本开⽀的约
36% 。
展望 2027 年，市场预计将有约 4400 亿美元来⾃超⼤规模云⼚商的直接发债，以及约 3000 亿美元
AI 项⽬融资进⼊市场，覆盖投资级债券、⾼收益债、私募信贷和结构化融资等多个渠道。

此外，超⼤规模云⼚商的表外承诺规模已⾼达 3.6 万亿美元，其中约 6850 亿美元将在 2027 年底前
到期。
这意味着， AI 融资的真正考验尚未到来。若未来利率维持⾼位、数据中⼼建设受阻、算⼒需求增
速放缓，或 GPU 更新速度快于资产回收速度，⻛险可能进⼀步从芯⽚商传导⾄项⽬融资、私募
信贷和⾼收益债市场。
⾼盛交易员 Avishek Biswas 认为，当前更像是 “ ⾼度不确定 ” ⽽⾮ “ 全⾯⾼⻛险 ” 的阶段。但市场显
然已开始提前定价尾部⻛险：指数或许仍显平静，芯⽚供应商和数据中⼼融资端的压⼒却已经
率先浮现。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
